6
Conclusions

In this work, we introduce a meta-algorithm for speeding up two estimators from non-parametric regression, namely the Nadaraya-Watson and Kernel Ridge Regression estimators. Our method inherits the favorable computational efficiency of the underlying Kernel Thinning algorithm and stands to benefit from further advancements in unsupervised learning compression methods.

The KT guarantees provided in this work apply only when
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})
for some base kernel
\mathbf{k}
.
In practice, choosing a good kernel
\mathbf{k}
is indeed a challenge common to all prior work.
Our framework is friendly to recent developments in kernel selection to handle this problem:

Dwivedi and Mackey [9, Cor. 1]
provide integration-error guarantees for KT when
f^{\star}\notin\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})
.
Moreover, there are recent results on finding the best kernel (e.g., for hypothesis testing
[
8
, Sec. 4.2]
).

Radhakrishnan et al. [20]
introduce the Recursive Feature Machine, which uses a parameterized kernel
\mathbf{k}_{M}(x_{1},x_{2})\triangleq\exp(-{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{\top}M(x_{1}-x_{2})}/{(2h^{2})})
, and propose an efficient method to learn the matrix parameter
M
via the average gradient outer product estimator. An exciting future direction would be to combine these parameterized (or "learned") kernels with our proposed KT methods for non-parametric regression.

7
Acknowledgements

AG is supported with funding from the NewYork-Presbyterian Hospital.

Appendix A
Background on
KT-Compress++

This section details the
KT-Compress++
algorithm of
Shetty et al. [23, Ex. 6]
.
In a nutshell,
KT-Compress
(
Alg.

2
) takes as input a point sequence of size
n
, a compression level
\mathfrak{g}
, a (reproducing) kernel functions
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
, and a failure probability
\delta
.

KT-Compress++
first runs the
\hyperref@@ii[algo:ktcompress]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{KT-Compress}}}(\mathfrak{g})
algorithm of
Shetty et al. [23, Ex. 4]
to produce an intermediate coreset of size
2^{\mathfrak{g}}\sqrt{n}
. Next, the KT algorithm is run on the intermediate coreset to produce a final output of size
\sqrt{n}
.

KT-Compress
proceeds by calling the recursive procedure
Compress
, which uses KT with kernels
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
as an intermediate halving algorithm.
The KT algorithm itself consists of two subroutines: (1)
kt-split
(
Alg.

3
), which splits a given input point sequence into two equal halves with small approximation error in the
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
reproducing kernel Hilbert space and (2)
kt-swap
(
Alg.

4
), which selects the best approximation amongst the
kt-split
coresets and a baseline coreset (that simply selects every other point in the sequence) and then iteratively refines the selected coreset by swapping out each element in turn for the non-coreset point that most improves
\mmd_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}
error.
As in
Shetty et al. [23, Rem. 3]
, we symmetrize the output of KT by returning either the KT coreset or its complement with equal probability.

Following
Shetty et al. [23, Ex. 6]
, we always default to
\mathfrak{g}=\lceil{\log_{2}\log n+3.1}\rceil
so that
KT-Compress++
has an overall runtime of
\mathcal{O}(n\log^{3}n)
. For the sake of simplicity, we drop any dependence on
\mathfrak{g}
in the main paper.

Algorithm 1

KT-Compress++
– Identify coreset of size
\sqrt{n}

Input:

point sequence
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
of size
n
, compression level
\mathfrak{g}
, kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
, failure probability
\delta

\mathcal{S}_{\textsc{C}}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\leftarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\hyperref@@ii[algo:ktcompress]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{KT-Compress}}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\delta)
// coreset of size
2^{\mathfrak{g}}\sqrt{n}

\mathcal{S}_{\textsc{C++}}\ \ \leftarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{KT}(\mathcal{S}_{\textsc{C}},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\mathfrak{g}+2^{\mathfrak{g}}(\beta_{n}+1)}\delta)
// coreset of size
\sqrt{n}

return

\mathcal{S}_{\textsc{C++}}

Algorithm 2

KT-Compress
– Identify coreset of size
2^{\mathfrak{g}}\sqrt{n}

Input:

point sequence
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
of size
n
, compression level
\mathfrak{g}
, kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
, failure probability
\delta

return

\textsc{Compress}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\frac{\delta}{n4^{\mathfrak{g}+1}(\log_{4}n-\mathfrak{g})})

function

Compress
(
\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\delta
)
:

if

|\mathcal{S}|=4^{\mathfrak{g}}

then

return

\mathcal{S}

Partition
\mathcal{S}
into four arbitrary subsequences
\{\mathcal{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{4}
each of size
n/4

for

i=1,2,3,4

do

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}_{i}}\leftarrow\textsc{Compress}(\mathcal{S}_{i},\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\delta)
// run
Compress
recursively to return coresets of size
2^{\mathfrak{g}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{|\mathcal{S}|}{4}}

end for

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}\leftarrow\textsc{Concatenate}(\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{2},\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{3},\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{4})
// combine the coresets to obtain a coreset of size
2\cdot 2^{\mathfrak{g}}\cdot\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}

return

\textsc{KT}(\widetilde{\mathcal{S}},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},|\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}|^{2}\delta)

// halve the coreset to size
2^{\mathfrak{g}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}
via symmetrized kernel thinning

function

KT
(
\mathcal{S},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\delta
)
:

// Identify kernel thinning coreset containing
\lfloor{|\mathcal{S}|/2}\rfloor
input points

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}\leftarrow\hyperref@@ii[algo:ktswap]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{kt-swap}}}(\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\hyperref@@ii[algo:ktsplit]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{kt-split}}}(\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\mathcal{S},\delta))

return

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}
with probability
\frac{1}{2}
and the complementary coreset
\mathcal{S}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}
otherwise

Algorithm 3

kt-split
–
Divide points into candidate coresets of size
\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor

Input:

kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}
, point sequence
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}=(x_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, failure probability
\delta

\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\mathcal{S}^{(2)}\leftarrow\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{}}\right\}
// Initialize empty coresets:
\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\mathcal{S}^{(2)}
have size
i
after round
i

\sigma\leftarrow 0
// Initialize swapping parameter

for

i=1,2,\ldots,\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor

do

// Consider two points at a time

(x,x^{\prime})\leftarrow(x_{2i-1},x_{2i})

// Compute swapping threshold
\mathfrak{a}_{i}

\mathfrak{a}_{i},\sigma\leftarrow
get_swap_params(
\sigma,\mathfrak{b},\frac{\delta}{n}
)

with
\mathfrak{b}^{2}\!=\!\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(x,x)\!+\!\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime})\!-\!2\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(x,x^{\prime})

// Assign one point to each coreset after probabilistic swapping

\theta\leftarrow\sum_{j=1}^{2i-2}(\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(x_{j},x)-\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(x_{j},x^{\prime}))-2\sum_{z\in\mathcal{S}^{(1)}}(\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(z,x)-\mathbf{k}_{\textrm{split}}(z,x^{\prime}))

[2pt]

(x,x^{\prime})\leftarrow(x^{\prime},x)

with probability

\min(1,\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{\theta}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}})_{+})

[2pt]

\mathcal{S}^{(1)}\texttt{.append}(x);\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{S}^{(2)}\texttt{.append}(x^{\prime})

end for

return

(\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\mathcal{S}^{(2)})
, candidate coresets of size
\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor

function

get_swap_params(
\sigma,\mathfrak{b},\delta
)
:

\mathfrak{a}_{i}\leftarrow\max(\mathfrak{b}\sigma\sqrt{\smash[b]{2\log(2/\delta)}},\mathfrak{b}^{2})

\sigma^{2}\leftarrow\sigma^{2}\!+\!\mathfrak{b}^{2}(1\!+\!({\mathfrak{b}^{2}}{}\!-\!2\mathfrak{a}_{i}){\sigma^{2}}{/\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}})_{+}

return

(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\sigma)

Algorithm 4

kt-swap
–
Identify and refine the best candidate coreset

Input:

kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
, point sequence
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}=(x_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, candidate coresets
(\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\mathcal{S}^{(2)})

\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\!\leftarrow\!\texttt{baseline\_coreset}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\texttt{size}\!=\!\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor)
// Compare to baseline (e.g., standard thinning)

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}\!\leftarrow\!\mathcal{S}^{(\ell^{\star})}\text{ for }\ell^{\star}\!\leftarrow\!\mathop{\mathrm{}}{argmin}_{\ell\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1,2}}\right\}}\mmd_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{S}^{(\ell)})

// Select best coreset

// Swap out each point in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}
for best alternative in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
while ensuring no point is repeated in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}

[1pt]

for

i=1,\ldots,\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor

do

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}[i]\leftarrow\mathop{\mathrm{}}{argmin}_{z\in\{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}[i]\}\cup(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}})}\mmd_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}\text{ with }\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}[i]=z)

end for

return

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}
, refined coreset of size
\lfloor{n/2}\rfloor

Define the event

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hyperref@@ii[app:ktcompresspp]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{KT-Compress++}}}~\text{succeeds}}}\right\}.

(33)

Dwivedi and Mackey [10, Thm. 1, Rmk. 4]
show that

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

(34)

We restate
[
10
, Thm. 4]
in our notation:

Lemma 1

(
L^{\infty}
guarantee for
KT-Compress++
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and consider a reproducing kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}:\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Assume
n/n_{\mathrm{out}}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}\triangleq\hyperref@@ii[app:ktcompresspp]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{KT-Compress++}}}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\delta)
and define
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\delta_{z}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}}\triangleq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\delta_{z}
. Then on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
, the following bound holds:

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq c\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty,\mathrm{in}}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},d,\delta,R),\quad\text{where}\quad

(35)

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},d,\delta,R)

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log_{2}(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)}\times

(36)

\textstyle\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)}+\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}(R_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},n}+R)}}\right)}}}\right],

(37)

\textstyle L_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}

\textstyle\triangleq\sup_{z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\in\mathcal{Z}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}(z_{1},z_{2})-\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}(z_{1},z_{3})}}\right|}{\|z_{2}-z_{3}\|_{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad

(38)

\textstyle R_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},n}

\textstyle\triangleq\inf\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r:\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}\\
\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{2}\geq r\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}(z_{1},z_{2})}}\right|\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}}}\right\},

(39)

for some universal positive constant
c
.

Proof.

The claim follows by replacing
\mathbf{k}

[
10
, Thm. 4]
with
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
, replacing the sub-Gaussian constant of KT with that of
KT-Compress++
in
[
23
, Ex. 5]
, and replacing
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty,\mathrm{in}}\triangleq\sup_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}(z,z)
throughout.
∎

We restate
[
9
, Thm. 2]
in our notation:

Lemma 2

(MMD guarantee for
KT-Compress++
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and consider a reproducing kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}:\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Assume
n/n_{\mathrm{out}}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}\triangleq\hyperref@@ii[app:ktcompresspp]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\textsc{KT-Compress++}}}(\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}},\mathfrak{g})(\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})
and define
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\delta_{z}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}}\triangleq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\delta_{z}
.
Then on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
, the following bound holds:

\textstyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}):\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{h}}}\right\|_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})h}}\right|

\textstyle\leq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\epsilon\in(0,1)\\
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}\subset\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{2\epsilon+\frac{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}}}\right\|_{\infty,\mathrm{in}}^{1/2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,\mathcal{A},\epsilon)}}\right\}\quad\text{where}\quad

(40)

\textstyle\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,R,\epsilon)

\textstyle\triangleq c\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\log\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}}(\mathcal{A},\epsilon)}}\right]}.

(41)

for some universal postive constant
c
.

Proof.

The claim follows from replacing
\mathbf{k}
in
[
9
, Thm. 2]
with
\mathbf{k}_{\textsc{Alg}}
and replacing the sub-Gaussian constant of KT with that of
KT-Compress++
in
[
23
, Ex. 5]
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of
Thm.

1
:
Theorem 1

(
KT-NW
)
.

Our primary goal is to bound
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}[(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0}))^{2}]
for a fixed
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
. Once we have this bound, bounding
\|\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}-f^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}
is as straightforward as integrating over
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
.

Consider the following decomposition:

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})+\widehat{f}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

(42)

\textstyle\leq 2~\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

(43)

\textstyle\qquad+2~\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}}}\right].

(44)

Define the random variables

\textstyle\eta_{i}\triangleq\mathbf{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{X_{i}-x_{0}}}}\right\|}{h}\leq 1}}\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,2,\ldots,n.

(45)

Also define the event

\textstyle\mathcal{E}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}>0}}\right\}.

(46)

Since
X_{i}
are i.i.d. samples from
\mathbb{P}
, it follows that
\eta_{i}
are i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables with parameter

\textstyle\overline{p}\triangleq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\eta_{i}=1}}}\right)\geq c_{0}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d},

(47)

where
c_{0}>0
depends only on
d
and
\kappa
(see
Assumption

2
).
Denote the denominator terms in
\widehat{f}
and
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}
by

\textstyle\widehat{p}(\cdot)\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot,x_{i}}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\cdot)\triangleq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot,x_{i}^{\prime}}}\right),

(48)

respectively, and the numerator terms in
\widehat{f}
and
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}
by

\textstyle\widehat{A}(\cdot)\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot,x_{i}}}\right)y_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\cdot)\triangleq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cdot,x_{i}^{\prime}}}\right)y_{i}^{\prime},

(49)

respectively.

We now consider two cases depending on the event
\mathcal{E}
.

Case I:
Suppose event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
is satisfied. It follows from
48
that
\widehat{p}(x_{0})=0
, in which case
\widehat{f}(x_{0})=0
. Since
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{out}}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
, it necessarily follows that
\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})=0
and
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})=0
. Thus, we can bound
43
and
44
by

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}^{c}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle=0\quad\text{and}\quad

(50)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}^{c}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(0-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}^{c}}}}\right]}}}\right]

(51)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(f^{\star}}}\right)^{2}(x_{0})\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}^{c}}}}\right)

(52)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(f^{\star}}}\right)^{2}(x_{0})(1-\overline{p})^{n}

(53)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(f^{\star}}}\right)^{2}(x_{0})\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-Cnh^{d}}}\right\}

(54)

for some positive constant
C
that does not depend on
n
. Note that these are low-order terms compared to the rest of the calculations, so we may ignore them in the final bound.

Case II:
Otherwise, we may assume event
\mathcal{E}
is satisfied. Let us first bound
43
. On event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}

33
, we claim that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]\leq\frac{Cd\log^{2}n}{nh^{2d}}\quad\text{whenever}\quad\overline{p}=\omega(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n).

(55)

We defer the proof to
Sec.

B.1
.

Letting
X\triangleq(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
and
Y\triangleq(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n})
denote the
x
and
y
components of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
, respectively, we can further decompose
44
by

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]

(56)

\textstyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right],

(57)

where the first RHS term corresponds to the variance and the second RHS term corresponds to the bias.
We claim that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle\leq\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-Cnh^{d}}}\right\}+\frac{C}{nh^{d}}}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad

(58)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle\leq C\cdot L_{f}^{2}h^{2\beta}\log^{2}n,

(59)

for some constant
C>0
that does not depend on either
n
or
h
. We defer the proofs to
Sec.

B.2
and
B.3
.
Combining
55
,
58
, and
59
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-f^{\star}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{Cd\log^{2}n}{nh^{2d}}}_{\text{KT bound}}+\underbrace{2\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(ne^{-Cnh^{d}}+\frac{C}{nh^{d}}}}\right)}_{\text{Variance bound}}+\underbrace{2CL_{f}^{2}h^{2\beta}\log^{2}n}_{\text{Bias bound}}.

(60)

Note that
h^{d}\leq 1
, so the
\frac{Cd\log^{2}n}{nh^{2d}}
term dominates the
\frac{C}{nh^{d}}
term. Thus, the optimal choice of bandwidth
h
comes from balancing

\textstyle\frac{C}{nh^{2d}}\sim 2L_{f}^{2}h^{2\beta}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad h=cn^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+2d}}.

(61)

Finally, we must verify our growth rate assumption on
\overline{p}
in
55
is satisfied. Since
\beta>0
, we have

\textstyle\overline{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:pover}}}{{\geq}}c_{0}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:nw-kt-h}}}{{=}}c_{0}^{\prime}n^{-\frac{d}{2\beta+2d}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\overline{p}}{\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n}=\infty.

(62)

Plugging
61
into
60
yields the advertised bound
25
.

B.1
Proof of claim
55

We first provide a generic result for approximating the numerator and denominator terms defined in
48
and
49
.

Lemma 3

(Simultaneous
L^{\infty}
bound using
KT-Compress++
with
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
)
.

Suppose
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
Assumption

2
. Given
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
, the following bounds hold on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
:

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\widehat{p}-\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq c_{p}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}(\log n+\log(1/\delta))

(63)

\textstyle\|\widehat{A}-\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq c_{p}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}(\log n+\log(1/\delta)),

(64)

where
c_{a},c_{p}>0
are constants that do not depend on
d
or
n
.

See
Sec.

B.1.1
for the proof.

In the sequel, we will simply treat the
\log(1/\delta)
term as a constant, meaning the
\log n
terms dominate in the expressions.

With this lemma in hand, let us prove the claim
55
.
Define the following events:

\textstyle\mathcal{A}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})=0}}\right\}\quad\text{}\quad\mathcal{B}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})\neq 0}}\right\}\quad\text{}\quad\mathcal{C}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\widehat{p}(x_{0})\geq\frac{\overline{p}}{2}}}\right\}.

(65)

On event
\mathcal{E}
, consider the following decomposition:

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{C}^{c}}}}\right]}}}\right]

(66)

\textstyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{C}}}}\right]}}}\right]

(67)

\textstyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{C}}}}\right]}}}\right].

(68)

Bounding
66
.

Note that almost surely, we have

\textstyle|\widehat{f}(x_{0})|\leq Y_{\mathrm{max}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})|\leq Y_{\mathrm{max}}.

(69)

Thus, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{C}^{c}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle\leq 4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}~\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({n~\widehat{p}(x_{0})<\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}}}}\right)

(70)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}-n\overline{p}<\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}-n\overline{p}}}}\right)

(71)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}c_{0}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{1}nh^{d}}}\right\},

(72)

where (i) follows from subtracting
n\overline{p}
from both sides of the probability statement and (ii) follows from concentration of Bernoulli random variables (see
Sec.

B.2
).

Bounding
67
.

Note that on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{C}
, we have

\textstyle\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})

\textstyle\geq\widehat{p}(x_{0})-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\widehat{p}-\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

(73)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}\frac{\overline{p}}{2}-c_{p}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n

(74)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:nadaraya-approximation}}}{{\geq}}c_{1}\overline{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:pover}}}{{\geq}}c_{2}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}>0.

(75)

where step (i) follows from applying
63
and substituting
\widehat{p}\geq\frac{\overline{p}}{2}
. Hence the events
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
and
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{C}
are mutually exclusive with probability
1
, thereby yielding

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{C}}}}\right]}}}\right]=0.

(76)

Bounding
68
.

On the event
\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{C}
, we have
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})=\frac{\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}
and
\widehat{f}(x_{0})=\frac{\widehat{A}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})}
, which yields

\textstyle\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})

\textstyle=\frac{\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}}{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}}-\frac{\widehat{A}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})}=\frac{\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}(x_{0})-\widehat{A}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}

(77)

\textstyle=\frac{(\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{A}(x_{0}))\cdot\widehat{p}(x_{0})+\widehat{A}(x_{0})\cdot(\widehat{p}(x_{0})-\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0}))}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}

(78)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{A}(x_{0})}}\right|\cdot\widehat{p}(x_{0})+\widehat{A}(x_{0})\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{p}(x_{0})-\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}}\right|}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}

(79)

We can invoke
63
and
64
to bound
|\widehat{p}(x_{0})-\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})|
and
|\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{A}(x_{0})|
respectively. Thus, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{C}}}}\right]}}}\right]

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c_{a}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n\cdot\widehat{p}(x_{0})+c_{p}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\log n\cdot\widehat{A}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})\cdot\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}

(80)

\textstyle\leq\frac{2d\cdot\log^{2}n}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c_{a}}{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\widehat{A}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c_{p}}{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]

(81)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2d\cdot\log^{2}n}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{c_{a}^{2}+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}c_{p}^{2}}{\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})}}}\right]^{2}

(82)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{Cd\log^{2}n}{nh^{2d}},

(83)

for some positive constant
C
that does not depend on
n
, where step (i) uses the fact that
\frac{\widehat{A}(x_{0})}{\widehat{p}(x_{0})}\leq Y_{\mathrm{max}}
and step (ii) uses the lower bound on
\widehat{p}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})
from
75
.
Combining
66
,
67
, and
68
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{0})-\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}}}}\right]}}}\right]\leq c_{0}~\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{1}nh^{d}}}\right\}+\frac{Cd\log n}{nh^{2d}}.

(84)

Note that the second term dominates so that we may drop the first term with slight change to the value of the constant
C
in the bound
55
.

B.1.1
Proof of
Lem.

3
:
Lemma 3

(Simultaneous
L^{\infty}
bound using
KT-Compress++
with
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
)
.

We first decompose
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
as

\textstyle\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))

\textstyle=\mathbf{k}_{1}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))+\mathbf{k}_{2}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})),\quad\text{where}\quad

(85)

\textstyle\mathbf{k}_{1}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad

(86)

\textstyle\mathbf{k}_{2}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot y_{1}y_{2}.

(87)

and note that

\textstyle\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}})=\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{2}).

(88)

This fact will be useful later for proving simultaneous
L^{\infty}
approximation guarantees for
\widehat{A}
and
\widehat{p}
.

Given that
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
Assumption

2
, we want to show that
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
defined by
19
satisfies the Lipschitz and tail decay properties, so that we may apply
Lem.

1
.
Note that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}).

(89)

We claim that kernel
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
satisfies

\textstyle L_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}

\textstyle\leq L_{\mathbf{k}}+Y_{\mathrm{max}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}+L_{\mathbf{k}}Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad

(90)

\textstyle R_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}},n}

\textstyle\leq R_{\mathbf{k},n}+2Y_{\mathrm{max}}

(91)

By
[
10
, Rmk. 8]
, we have

\textstyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\leq\frac{L_{\kappa}}{h}\quad\text{and}\quad R_{\mathbf{k},n}\leq h\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n),

(92)

where
\kappa^{\dagger}
is defined by
22
.
Applying
89
and
90
, we have

\textstyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}+Y_{\mathrm{max}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}+L_{\mathbf{k}}Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}\right)}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2})}

(93)

\textstyle\leq\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{Y_{\mathrm{max}}}+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}

(94)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:L_k-R_k}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2L_{\kappa}}{h}+\frac{1}{Y_{\mathrm{max}}}.

(95)

Finally, we have

\textstyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}R_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}},n}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2L_{\kappa}}{h}+\frac{1}{Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(h\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)+2Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}\right)

(96)

\textstyle=2L_{\kappa}\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)+\frac{4L_{\kappa}Y_{\mathrm{max}}}{h}+\frac{h\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)}{Y_{\mathrm{max}}}+2

(97)

\textstyle\leq 4\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1,L_{\kappa}Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}\right\}\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)}{h}

(98)

\textstyle\leq 4\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1,L_{\kappa}Y_{\mathrm{max}}}}\right\}\cdot c^{\prime}n^{\alpha},

(99)

where the last inequality follows from
Assumption

2
for some universal positive constant
c^{\prime}
.

Since
Assumption

1
is satisfied,
R
is constant. Applying
99
to
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},d,\delta,R)
as defined by
36
, we have the bound

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},d,\delta,R)\leq c^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}}{\delta}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{8}{\delta}}}\right)}+5\sqrt{d\log n}}}\right]

(100)

for some positive constant
c^{\prime\prime}
. Substituting this into
35
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq c_{1}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2})}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}}{\delta}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{8}{\delta}}}\right)}+5\sqrt{d\log n}}}\right]

(101)

\textstyle\leq c_{2}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2})}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sqrt{d}(\sqrt{\log n}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})^{2},

(102)

for some positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}
.
By definition,

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\langle{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}},{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(\cdot,z)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}.

(103)

Define
\mathbf{k}_{1}
and
\mathbf{k}_{2}
by
86
and
87
, respectively, and note that
\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,z),\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,z)\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}})
for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
. We want to show that

\textstyle\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\langle{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}},{\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,z)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}

\textstyle\leq c_{2}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2})}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sqrt{d}(\sqrt{\log n}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad

(104)

\textstyle\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\langle{(\mathbb{P}_{\textup{in}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\textup{out}})\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}},{\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,z)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}

\textstyle\leq c_{2}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(1+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2})}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sqrt{d}(\sqrt{\log n}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})^{2},

(105)

which would imply
63
and
64
(after simplifying all terms besides
n
,
d
, and
\delta
).

The first inequality follows from replacing all occurrences of the test function
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(\cdot,(x,y))
in the proof of
Lem.

1
with the function
\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,x)
and noting that
\langle{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(\cdot,(x_{i},y_{i}))},{\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,(x,y))}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}=\langle{\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,x_{i})},{\mathbf{k}_{1}(\cdot,x)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}
from the fact that
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}})=\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

88
.

The second inequality follows from replacing all occurrences of the test function
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(\cdot,(x,y))
in the proof of
Lem.

1
with the function
\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,x)
and noting that
\langle{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(\cdot,(x_{i},y_{i}))},{\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,(x,y))}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}=\langle{\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,(x_{i},y_{i}))},{\mathbf{k}_{2}(\cdot,(x,y))}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}
, again from the fact that
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}})=\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

88
.

Proof of claim
90
.

We leverage the fact that the Lipschitz constants defined by
38
satisfies the following additivity property.
Letting
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
, we have

\textstyle L_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}

\textstyle=\sup_{z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\in\mathcal{Z}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(z_{1},z_{2})-\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}(z_{1},z_{3})}}\right|}{\|{z_{2}-z_{3}}\|_{2}}

(106)

\textstyle\leq\sup_{z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\in\mathcal{Z}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})-\mathbf{k}_{1}(z_{1},z_{3})}}\right|}{\|{z_{2}-z_{3}}\|_{2}}+\sup_{z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\in\mathcal{Z}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}_{2}(z_{1},z_{2})-\mathbf{k}_{2}(z_{1},z_{3})}}\right|}{\|{z_{2}-z_{3}}\|_{2}}

(107)

\textstyle=L_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}+L_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}.

(108)

We proceed to bound
L_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}
and
L_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}
separately. Note that

\textstyle L_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}=L_{\mathbf{k}}.

(109)

Applying the definition
38
to
L_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}
, we have

\textstyle L_{\mathbf{k}_{2}}

\textstyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1})\\
z_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2})\\
z_{3}=(x_{3},y_{3})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})y_{1}y_{2}-\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{3})y_{1}y_{3}}}\right|}{\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{2}-x_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{2}-y_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}}}

(110)

\textstyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1})\\
z_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2})\\
z_{3}=(x_{3},y_{3})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|y_{1}}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})y_{2}-\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})y_{3}+\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})y_{3}-\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{3})y_{3}}}\right|}{\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{2}-x_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{2}-y_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}}}

(111)

\textstyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1})\\
z_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2})\\
z_{3}=(x_{3},y_{3})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|y_{1}}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})(y_{2}-y_{3})+(\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})-\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{3}))y_{3}}}\right|}{\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{2}-x_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{2}-y_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}}}

(112)

\textstyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1})\\
z_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2})\\
z_{3}=(x_{3},y_{3})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|y_{1}}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})(y_{2}-y_{3})}}\right|}{\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{2}-x_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{2}-y_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}}}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1})\\
z_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2})\\
z_{3}=(x_{3},y_{3})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|y_{1}}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})-\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{3}))y_{3}}}\right|}{\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{2}-x_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{2}-y_{3}}}}\right\|^{2}}}

(113)

\textstyle\leq Y_{\mathrm{max}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}+L_{\mathbf{k}}Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}.

(114)

Putting together the pieces yields the claimed bound.

Proof of claim
91
.

We aim to show that
R_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}},n}
is not much larger than
R_{\mathbf{k},n}
. Note that
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}
can be rewritten as

\textstyle\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))=(1+y_{1}y_{2})\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2}).

(115)

We define the sets

\textstyle\Gamma

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r:\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},x_{2}:\\
\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|_{2}\geq r\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})}}\right|\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}}}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad

(116)

\textstyle\Gamma^{\star}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r^{\star}:\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}):\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{1}-x_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{1}-y_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}\geq(r^{\star})^{2}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot y_{1}y_{2}}}\right|\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}}}\right\},

(117)

noting that

\textstyle R_{\mathbf{k},n}=\inf\Gamma\quad\text{and}\quad R_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}},n}=\inf\Gamma^{\star}

(118)

by definition
39
.

Suppose
r\in\Gamma
. Then for any
(x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})
such that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{1}-x_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{1}-y_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}\geq r^{2}+4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2},

(119)

it must follow that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{1}-x_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}\geq r^{2}
(since
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y_{1}-y_{2}}}}\right\|^{2}\leq 4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}
by triangle inequality). Since
r
satisfies
116
, it must follow that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot(y_{1}y_{2}+1)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{1},x_{2})}}\right|(Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}+1)\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}(Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}+1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:kernelnw-infnorm}}}{{=}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n},

(120)

meaning
\sqrt{r^{2}+4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}}\in\Gamma^{\star}
, where recall
\Gamma^{\star}
is defined by
117
. Thus, we have

\textstyle R_{\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{NW}},n}\leq\sqrt{R_{\mathbf{k},n}+4Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}}\leq R_{\mathbf{k},n}+2Y_{\mathrm{max}}

(121)

as desired.

B.2
Proof of claim
58

Suppose event
\mathcal{E}

46
is satisfied. Define the shorthand for the variance:

\textstyle\sigma^{2}(x_{0};X)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{f}(x_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]}}\right)^{2}}}}\right].

(122)

Conditioned on
X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},\ldots,X_{n}=x_{n}
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{Y_{1}\mid X_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}\mid X_{n}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}}\right]=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{\star}(X_{i})\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})},

(123)

where we have used the fact that
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Y\mid X=\cdot}}}\right]=f^{\star}(\cdot)
by assumption
2
.

Note that on event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\textstyle\sigma^{2}(x_{0};X)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:cond-mean}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{\star}(X_{i})\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

(124)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

(125)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}[v_{1}]\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})^{2}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}\right)^{2}},

(126)

where recall
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
are i.i.d. random variables with
\mathrm{Var}[v_{i}]=\sigma^{2}
by
2
. Taking the expectation w.r.t.
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and leveraging symmetry, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sigma^{2}(x_{0};X)~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]=n\sigma^{2}\cdot\sigma_{X}^{2},\quad\text{where}\quad\sigma_{X}^{2}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}(X_{1},x_{0})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}}}}\right].

(127)

\sigma_{X}^{2}
can be bounded by

\textstyle\sigma_{X}^{2}

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}(X_{1},x_{0})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\leq\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}}}}\right]}}}\right]+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{n\overline{p}}}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{k}^{2}(X_{1},x_{0})}}}\right]

(128)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\leq\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{n\overline{p}}}}\right)^{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{k}^{2}(x_{1},x_{0})p(dx_{1}),

(129)

where step (i) follows from the fact that
\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}(X_{1},x_{0})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}\right)^{2}}\leq 1
.
Using Bernstein’s inequality
[
29
, Prop. 2.14]
, the first term can be bounded by

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\leq\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}}}}\right)

\textstyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}-n\overline{p}\leq-\frac{n\overline{p}}{2}}}}\right)

(130)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leq}}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-\frac{(n\overline{p})^{2}}{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\overline{p}(1-\overline{p})+n\overline{p}/3}}\right)}}}\right\}\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{1}nh^{d}}}\right\}

(131)

for some universal positive constant
c
.
Applying the fact that
p
is bounded by
Assumption

1
and
\kappa
is square-integrable by
Assumption

2
, we can bound the second term by

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{n\overline{p}}}}\right)^{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{k}^{2}(x_{1},x_{0})p(dx_{1})

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{n\overline{p}}}}\right)^{2}p_{\max}h^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\kappa^{2}(u)du

(132)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{4}{(n\overline{p})^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(c_{1}h^{d}}}\right)\leq\frac{c_{2}}{n^{2}h^{d}},

(133)

for some positive constant
c_{2}
that does not depend on either
h
or
n
. Substituting these expressions into
127
yields a bound on
\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sigma^{2}(x_{0};X)~\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathcal{E}}}}\right]}}}\right]
as desired.

B.3
Proof of claim
59

Define the following shorthand for the bias:

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{Y\mid X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\widehat{f}(x_{0})}}}\right]-f^{\star}(x_{0}).

(134)

We state a more detailed version of the claim.

Lemma 4

(Bias of Nadaraya-Watson)
.

Suppose
1
and
2
are satisfied and the event
\mathcal{E}

46
holds.
If
f^{\star}\in\Sigma(\beta,L_{f})
for
\beta\in(0,1]
,
L_{f}>0
, then with high probability, the following statements hold true uniformly for all
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
:

(I.a)

If
\mathbf{k}
is compactly supported, then
b(x_{0};X)\leq L_{f}h^{\beta}
.

(I.b)

Otherwise, if
\mathbf{k}
is non-compactly supported, we have
b(x_{0};X)\leq c\cdot L_{f}h^{\beta}\log n
for some positive constant
c
that does not depend on
n
.

For completeness, we first state the proof of claim
(I.a)
from
Belkin et al. [3, Lem. 2]
.
On the event
\mathcal{E}

46
, we have

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:cond-mean}}}{{=}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f^{\star}(X_{i})-f^{\star}(x_{0}))\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{f}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{X_{i}}}}\right\|^{\beta}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}L_{f}h^{\beta},

(135)

where step (i) follows from our assumption that
f^{\star}\in\Sigma(\beta,L_{f})
and step (ii) follows from our assumption that
\mathbf{k}
is compactly supported, so
\mathbf{k}(x,x_{0})=0
whenever
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x_{0}}}}\right\|>h
. We now proceed to proving claim
(I.b)
.

Step I: Decomposing the bias.

For any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
, define the random variables

\textstyle A_{0}(x_{0})

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad

(136)

\textstyle A_{j}(x_{0})

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T,

(137)

where

\textstyle T\triangleq\lceil{\frac{\log R}{\log h}}\rceil=\mathcal{O}(\log n)

(138)

when
p_{X}(\cdot)
has compact support under
Assumption

1
and
h
is defined by
61
. We call these random variables the
kernel empirical means
. We can directly verify that

\textstyle\sum_{j=0}^{T}A_{0}(x_{0})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\quad\text{for all}\quad x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}.

(139)

With these definitions in hand, we may rewrite
b(x_{0};X)
in terms of
A_{0}(x_{0}),A_{1}(x_{0}),\ldots,A_{T}(x_{0})
as follows:

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)

\textstyle=\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})(f^{\star}(X_{i})-f^{\star}(x_{0}))}{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}

(140)

\textstyle=\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right](f^{\star}(X_{i})-f^{\star}(x_{0}))}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}

(141)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right](f^{\star}(X_{i})-f^{\star}(x_{0}))}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}

(142)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right]L_{f}\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}

(143)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]L_{f}\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}

(144)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right]L_{f}h^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}

(145)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]L_{f}(2^{j}h)^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})},

(146)

where step (i) follows from the fact that
f^{\star}(X_{i})-f^{\star}(x_{0})\leq L_{f}\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|^{\beta}
by
Def.

1
and step (ii) follows from the fact that
\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h
under the indicator functions. Applying
136
to
145
and
137
to
146
, we have

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)

\textstyle\leq\frac{A_{0}(x_{0})L_{f}h^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}+\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})L_{f}(2^{j}h)^{\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}\leq L_{f}h^{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T}\frac{A_{i}(x_{0})2^{i\beta}}{\sum_{j=0}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}}}\right).

(147)

For any
z\in\mathcal{X}
, define the following shorthands:

\textstyle a_{0}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{k}(X,z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X-z\|\leq h}}}\right]}}}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad

(148)

\textstyle a_{j}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{k}(X,x)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X-z\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]}}}\right]\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T.

(149)

We can directly verify that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{A_{0}(z)}}}\right]=a_{0}(z)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{A_{j}(z)}}}\right]=a_{j}(z)\quad\text{for all}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T.

(150)

Under
Assumption

1
,
a_{i}(z)
can be computed directly from the decay properties of
\mathbf{k}
as follows:

\textstyle a_{0}(z)

\textstyle=\int_{\|x-z\|\leq h}\mathbf{k}(x,z)p_{X}(dx)\geq p_{\mathrm{\min}}\int_{\|x-z\|\leq h}\mathbf{k}(x,z)dx

(151)

\textstyle a_{j}(z)

\textstyle=\int_{2^{j-1}<\|x-z\|\leq 2^{j}h}\mathbf{k}(x,z)p_{X}(dx)\leq p_{\max}\int_{2^{j-1}<\|x-z\|\leq 2^{j}h}\mathbf{k}(x,z)dx\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T.

(152)

Remark 1
.

Note that for fixed
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
,
A_{i}(x_{0})
, Hoeffding’s inequality implies that
A_{i}(x_{0})
concentrates around
a_{i}(x_{0})
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,T
with high probability. Assuming that
\mathbf{k}
decays sufficiently rapidly (so that
a_{j}(x_{0})2^{j\beta}=\mathcal{O}(a_{0}(x_{0}))
for all
j\in[T]
), we have

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)\leq L_{f}h^{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{T}O(1)}}\right)=\mathcal{O}(L_{f}h^{\beta}\log n).

(153)

It remains to show that this bound holds uniformly over all
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with high probability.

Step II: Uniform concentration of empirical kernel means.

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{h}{2}]
and let
\mathcal{C}_{0}
be a
\epsilon
-cover of
\mathcal{X}
w.r.t. the Euclidean norm (see
[
29
, Def. 5.1]
for the detailed definition). We will optimize the choice of
\epsilon
at the end of the proof. By
[
29
, Lem. 5.7]
, we have

\textstyle|\mathcal{C}_{0}|\leq(1+\frac{2R}{\epsilon})^{d}

(154)

We now introduce a slightly modified version of
148
and
149
defined as

\textstyle a_{0}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{k}(X,z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X-z\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]}}}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad

(155)

\textstyle a_{j}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{k}(X,z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X-z\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]}}}\right]\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T,

(156)

respectively. Using the fact that
\epsilon\leq h/2
,
1
and
2
, and a change of variables, we have

\textstyle a_{0}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\geq p_{\mathrm{\min}}\int_{\|x-z\|\leq\frac{h}{2}}\mathbf{k}(x,z)dx\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du,\quad\text{and}\quad

(157)

\textstyle a_{j}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\leq p_{\max}\int_{(2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2})h<\|x-z\|\leq(2^{j}+\frac{1}{2})h}\mathbf{k}(x,x_{0}^{\prime})dx\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}p_{\max}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2}}^{2^{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du\quad\text{for}\quad j\in[T],

(158)

where
\omega_{d-1}\triangleq 2\pi^{d/2}/\Gamma(d/2)
denotes the surface area of the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Moreover, we consider the following concentration events:

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-z\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]\geq a_{0}^{\epsilon}(z)-\Delta_{0}}}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad

(159)

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{j}^{\epsilon}(z)

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-z\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]\leq a_{j}^{\epsilon}(z)+\Delta_{j}}}\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad j\in[T],

(160)

where
\Delta_{0},\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{T}
are positive scalars to be chosen later.

For any scalars
0<a<b
and
z\in\mathcal{X}
, further denote the empirical mass contained within the ball or annulus centered at point
z
by

\textstyle\mathrm{Mass}_{z}(a)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-z\|\leq a}}}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad

(161)

\textstyle\mathrm{Mass}_{z}(a,b)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{a<\|X_{i}-z\|\leq b}}}\right].

(162)

We now introduce a lemma that bounds the empirical kernel mean restricted to each ball and annuli:

Lemma 5

(Uniform concentration of empirical kernel means)
.

Suppose event
\bigcap_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}\bigcap_{i=0}^{T}\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\epsilon}(z)
is satisfied. Then for every
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
, there exists
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that
\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq\epsilon
and the following bounds hold simultaneously:

\textstyle A_{0}(x_{0})

\textstyle\geq a_{0}^{\epsilon}(x_{0}^{\prime})-\Delta_{0}-\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(h-\epsilon)\cdot L_{\mathbf{k}}\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad

(163)

\textstyle A_{j}(x_{0})

\textstyle\leq a_{j}^{\epsilon}(x_{0}^{\prime})+\Delta_{j}+\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)\cdot\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon}{h})\quad\text{for}\quad j\in[T].

(164)

Here,
L_{\mathbf{k}}
and
\kappa(\cdot)
are properties of
\mathbf{k}
from
Assumption

2
.

See
Sec.

B.3.1
for the proof.

This result suggests that we can control the error between the empirical kernel mean,
A_{i}(x_{0})
, and its (shifted) population counterpart,
a_{j}^{\epsilon}(x_{0}^{\prime})
, by choosing
\epsilon
to be small and leveraging the decay property of
\kappa
. To bound the
\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(h-\epsilon)
and
\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)
terms, we define the following events:

\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{0}(x_{0}^{\prime})

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(h-\epsilon)\leq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(h-\epsilon)}}}\right]+t_{0}}}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad

(165)

\textstyle\mathcal{M}_{j}(x_{0}^{\prime})

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)\leq\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)}}}\right]+t_{j}}}\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad j\in[T],

(166)

where
t_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{T}
are positive scalars to be chosen later.
For any scalars
0<a<b
, we may apply
Assumption

1
to obtain

\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(a)}}}\right]\leq p_{\max}\mathrm{Vol}(a)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(a,b)}}}\right]\leq p_{\max}\mathrm{Vol}(b),

(167)

where
\mathrm{Vol}(r)\triangleq\pi^{d}/\Gamma(d/2+1)r^{d}
denotes the volume of the Euclidean ball
\mathbb{B}_{2}(r)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Step III: Putting things together.

For any
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, note that

\textstyle\mathbf{k}(X_{i},z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-z\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]\in[0,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}]\quad\text{and}\quad

(168)

\textstyle\mathbf{k}(X_{i},z)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-z\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]\in[0,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}]

(169)

and therefore these random variables are sub-Gaussian with parameter
\sigma=\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
. Thus, we may bound
159
and
160
using Hoeffding’s bound
[
29
, Prop. 2.5]
, obtaining

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\epsilon}(z)^{c}}}}\right)\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-\frac{2n\Delta_{0}^{2}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}}}}\right\}\quad\text{for all}\quad i=0,1,\ldots,T\quad\text{and}\quad z\in\mathcal{C}_{0},

(170)

Again by Hoeffding’s bound
[
29
, Prop. 2.5]
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{M}_{i}(z)^{c}}}}\right)\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-2nt_{i}^{2}}}\right\}\quad\text{for all}\quad i=0,1,\ldots,T\quad\text{and}\quad z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}.

(171)

Next, it remains to choose the values of
\epsilon
,
\Delta_{0},\ldots,\Delta_{T}
and
t_{0},\ldots,t_{T}
. Consider

\textstyle\Delta_{0}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du,

(172)

\textstyle\Delta_{j}

\textstyle=2^{-j\beta}\cdot p_{\max}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T,

(173)

\textstyle t_{0}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}p_{\max}\mathrm{Vol}(h-\epsilon),\quad\text{and}\quad

(174)

\textstyle t_{j}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}p_{\max}\mathrm{Vol}(2^{j}h+\epsilon)\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,\ldots,T.

(175)

Combining
163
,
165
,
167
,
172
, and
174
, we have on the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\triangleq\bigcap_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\epsilon}(z)\cap\mathcal{M}_{0}(z))
the following bound:

\textstyle A_{0}(x_{0})

\textstyle\geq\frac{1}{2}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du-p_{\max}\frac{\pi^{d}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}(h-\epsilon)^{d}\cdot L_{k}\epsilon

(176)

\textstyle\geq h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}p_{\mathrm{\min}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du-p_{\max}\frac{\pi^{d}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)\omega_{d-1}}L_{\mathbf{k}}\epsilon}}\right)

(177)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{4}p_{\mathrm{\min}}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du,

(178)

where step (i) follows from setting

\textstyle\epsilon=\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[p_{\mathrm{\min}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}}\right]\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p_{\max}\frac{\pi^{d}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)\omega_{d-1}}L_{\mathbf{k}}}}\right)^{-1},\frac{h}{2}}}\right\}.

(179)

Similarly, for each
j=1,2,\ldots,T
, we may combine
164
,
166
,
167
,
173
, and
175
to obtain a bound on
A_{j}(x_{0})
. Hence, on the
\mathcal{E}_{j}\triangleq\bigcap_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{\epsilon}(z)\cap\mathcal{M}_{j}(z))
, we have

\textstyle A_{j}(x_{0})

\textstyle\leq p_{\max}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2}}^{2^{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du+2^{-j\beta}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}}\right)+p_{\max}\frac{\pi^{d}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}(2^{j}h+\epsilon)^{d}\cdot\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon}{h})

(180)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}p_{\max}h^{d}\omega_{d-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2}}^{2^{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du+2^{-j\beta}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du+\frac{\pi^{d}(2^{j}+\frac{1}{2})^{d}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)\omega_{d-1}}\kappa(2^{j-1}-1)}}\right)

(181)

where step (i) employs the fact that
\epsilon\leq h/2
. Note that for
j=1,2,\ldots,T
:

\textstyle\frac{A_{j}(x_{0})2^{j\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})}

\textstyle\leq\frac{4p_{\max}}{p_{\mathrm{\min}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2^{j\beta}\int_{2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2}}^{2^{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du+\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du+\frac{\pi^{d}(2^{j}+\frac{1}{2})^{d}2^{j\beta}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)\omega_{d-1}}\kappa(2^{j-1}-1)}{\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}(1),

(182)

where the last inequality comes from the fact that

\textstyle\frac{2^{j\beta}\int_{2^{j-1}-\frac{1}{2}}^{2^{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}{\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}=\mathcal{O}(1)\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\frac{\pi^{d}(2^{j}+\frac{1}{2})^{d}2^{j\beta}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)\omega_{d-1}}\kappa(2^{j-1}-1)}{\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa(u)u^{d-1}du}=\mathcal{O}(1)

(183)

by
Assumption

2
. Substituting this bound into
147
, we have

\textstyle b(x_{0};X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:bias-sum}}}{{\leq}}L_{f}h^{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T}\frac{A_{i}(x_{0})2^{j\beta}}{\sum_{j=0}^{T}A_{j}(x_{0})}}}\right)\leq L_{f}h^{\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T}\frac{A_{i}(x_{0})2^{j\beta}}{A_{0}(x_{0})}}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:Aj-A0}}}{{\leq}}L_{f}h^{\beta}TC\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:T}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{O}(L_{f}h^{\beta}\log n)

(184)

as desired. Finally, it remains to control the probability that this bound on
b(x_{0};X)
holds. Applying union-bound, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{0}^{c}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{E}_{T}^{c}}}}\right)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:cover0-size},\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:conc-Ai-eps}}}{{\leq}}(1+\frac{2R}{\epsilon})^{d}\sum_{i=0}^{T}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-\frac{2n\Delta_{i}^{2}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}}}}\right\}+\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-2nt_{i}^{2}}}\right\}}}\right)

(185)

\textstyle\leq(1+\frac{2R}{\epsilon})^{d}\sum_{j=0}^{T}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{1}nh^{2d}4^{-j\beta}}}\right\}+\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{2}nh^{jd}}}\right\}}}\right)

(186)

\textstyle\leq(1+\frac{2R}{\epsilon})^{d}(T+1)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{1}nh^{2d}4^{-T\beta}}}\right\}+\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-c_{2}nh^{Td}}}\right\}}}\right),

(187)

for some universal positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}
that do not depend on
n
. Note that by
179
, we have
(1+\frac{2R}{\epsilon})^{d}=\mathcal{O}((1+\frac{1}{h})^{d})
. Setting
h=cn^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+2d}}
as in
61
and recalling that
T=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
, we have
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{0}^{c}\cup\ldots\cup\mathcal{E}_{T}^{c}}}}\right)\ll 1
for sufficiently large
n
. This completes the proof of
Lem.

4
.

B.3.1
Proof of
Lem.

5
:
Lemma 5

(Uniform concentration of empirical kernel means)
.

Fix any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
. By definition of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, there exists
x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that
\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq\epsilon
. Consider any
x\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(h-\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime})
4
4

4

We use the notation
\mathbb{B}_{2}(r;x)
to denote the Euclidean ball of radius
r
centered at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
. By triangle inequality,
\|x-x_{0}\|\leq\|x-x_{0}^{\prime}\|+\|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}\|\leq(h-\epsilon)+\epsilon
. Therefore, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{B}_{2}(h-\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime})\subset\mathbb{B}_{2}(h;x_{0}).

(188)

We now introduce the following lemma, which is a finite-sample version of
[
10
, Lem. 8]
:

Lemma 6

(
L^{\infty}
bound on
{L}^{2}
kernel error (finite sample))
.

Consider any bounded kernel
\mathbf{k}\in L^{2,\infty}
, points
x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
, and function
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})
. For any
r,a,b\geq 0
with
a+b=1
, points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{X}
, and set
A\subset\mathcal{X}
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(189)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\|g\|_{A,L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}\mathrm{Mass}_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)+2\tau_{\mathbf{k},A}(ar)+2\|\mathbf{k}\|_{A,L^{2,\infty}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\geq br}}}\right]}}\right].

(190)

where

\textstyle\|f\|_{A,L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{2}(x_{i})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(191)

\textstyle\|f\|_{A,\infty}

\textstyle\triangleq\max_{i\in[n]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|f(x_{i})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(192)

\textstyle\mathrm{Mass}_{A}(r)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq r,x_{i}\in A}}}\right]

(193)

\textstyle\tau_{A,\mathbf{k}}(r)

\textstyle\triangleq\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}^{2}(x_{i},x^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\prime}\|\geq r,x_{i}\in A}}}\right]}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(194)

\textstyle\|\mathbf{k}\|_{A,L^{2,\infty}}

\textstyle\triangleq\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x^{\prime})\|_{A,L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

(195)

See
Sec.

B.3.2
for the proof.

We now use this lemma to prove each of the claims in
Lem.

5
.

Proof of claim
163
.

We consider the following basic decomposition:

\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right]

(196)

\textstyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]

(197)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq h}}}\right]}}\right).

(198)

Note that the term
197
can be directly bounded using the definition of
159
. Next, we bound the term
198
. Define

\textstyle g\triangleq\mathbf{1}\quad\text{and}\quad A\triangleq\mathbb{B}_{2}(h-\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime}),

(199)

so that
\|g\|_{A,L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h-\epsilon)
. By property
188
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}}\|\leq h}}}\right]\quad\text{for all}\quad i=1,2,\ldots,n.

(200)

Observe that

\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})

(201)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})-\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})}}\right)\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]

(202)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]

(203)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:indicator-bound}}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}\|\leq h-\epsilon}}}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|{X_{i}-x_{0}}\|\leq h}}}\right],

(204)

To upper bound
201
, we now apply
Lem.

6
with
r=h
,
a=\frac{1}{2}
, and
b=\frac{1}{2}
to obtain

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(205)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h-\epsilon)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}\mathrm{Mass}_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h)+2\tau_{\mathbf{k},A}(\frac{h}{2})+2\|\mathbf{k}\|_{A,L^{2,\infty}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|>\frac{h}{2}}}}\right]}}\right]

(206)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h-\epsilon)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[L_{\mathbf{k}}\epsilon\cdot\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h-\epsilon)}}\right],

(207)

where the last inequality uses the fact that

\textstyle\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}\leq L_{\mathbf{k}}\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq L_{\mathbf{k}}\epsilon

(208)

from the Lipschitz property of
\mathbf{k}
(see
Assumption

2
) and the fact that

\textstyle\mathrm{Mass}_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h)=\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(h-\epsilon),\quad\text{}\quad\tau_{\mathbf{k},A}(\frac{h}{2})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\|x_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq\epsilon\leq\frac{h}{2}.

(209)

Substituting the bounds
159
,
201
, and
207
into the decomposition
196
yields the desired claim.

Proof of claim
164
.

We consider the basic decomposition:

\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]

(210)

\textstyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]

(211)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]

(212)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right])

(213)

Note that the term
211
can be directly bounded using the definition of
160
. Next, we bound the term
213
. By triangle inequality, we have

\textstyle\|x-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq\|x-x_{0}\|+\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon,\quad\text{for all}\quad x\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(2^{j}h;x_{0}),

(214)

which implies that
\mathbb{B}_{2}(2^{j}h;x_{0})\subset\mathbb{B}_{2}(2^{j}h+\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime})
. Moreover, we can verify that

. Hence,

\textstyle\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]\quad\text{for all}\quad i=1,2,\ldots,n.

(215)

Now define

\textstyle g\triangleq 1\quad\text{and}\quad A\triangleq\mathbb{B}_{2}(2^{j}h+\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime})\setminus\mathbb{B}_{2}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon;x_{0}^{\prime}),

(216)

so that
\|g\|_{A,{L}^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)
.
Hence,
213
can be bounded by

\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h<\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\leq 2^{j}h}}}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]

(217)

\textstyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:annulus-indicator-bound}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]

(218)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon<\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\leq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]

(219)

\textstyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:g-A}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})-\mathbf{k}(X_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})

(220)

\textstyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leq}}\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon)\cdot\big[\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}\mathrm{Mass}_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}(2(2^{j}h+\epsilon))

(221)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+2\tau_{\mathbf{k},A}(2^{j}h+\epsilon)+2\|\mathbf{k}\|_{A,L^{2,\infty}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|\geq 2^{j}h+\epsilon}}}\right]\big]

(222)

where the last inequality follows from applying
Lem.

6
with

and
a=b=\frac{1}{2}
.
By triangle inequality, we have

\textstyle\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|\geq\|X_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|-\|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon)-\epsilon\geq 2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon,

(223)

where step (i) follows from the definition of
A

216
. Since
\kappa
is monotonically decreasing by
Assumption

2
, we have
\kappa(\frac{\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|}{h})\leq\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon}{h})
. Thus, we have

\textstyle\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}

\textstyle\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})\|_{A,\infty},\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime})\|_{A,\infty}}}\right\}

(224)

\textstyle\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon}{h}),\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-\epsilon}{h})}}\right\}

(225)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\kappa(\frac{2^{j-1}h-2\epsilon}{h}),

(226)

where step (i) again uses the fact that
\kappa
is monotonically decreasing. Moreover, note that

\textstyle\mathrm{Mass}_{A}(2(2^{j}h+\epsilon))

\textstyle=\mathrm{Mass}_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(2^{j-1}h-\epsilon,2^{j}h+\epsilon),

(227)

\textstyle\tau_{A,\mathbf{k}}(2^{j}h+\epsilon)

\textstyle=0,\quad\text{and}\quad

(228)

\textstyle\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|

\textstyle\leq\epsilon<2^{j}h+\epsilon.

(229)

Substituting the bounds

,

, and

into the decomposition

yields the desired claim.

B.3.2
Proof of
Lem.

6
:
Lemma 6

(
L^{\infty}
bound on
{L}^{2}
kernel error (finite sample))
.

Define the restrictions

\textstyle g_{r}(x)=g(x)\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(r;x_{0}^{\prime})}(x),\quad\text{}\quad g_{r}^{(c)}=g-g_{r},\quad\text{}\quad\mathbf{k}_{r}(x,z)\triangleq\mathbf{k}(x,z)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(r;x_{0}^{\prime})}(z),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}\triangleq\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}_{r},

(230)

so that
\mathbf{k}(z,x_{0})=\mathbf{k}_{r}(z,x_{0})+\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(z,x_{0})
and
\mathbf{k}(z,x_{0}^{\prime})=\mathbf{k}_{r}(z,x_{0}^{\prime})+\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(z,x_{0}^{\prime})
