. We now apply the triangle inequality and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(231)

\textstyle\qquad=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}_{r}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}_{r}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(232)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}_{r}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}_{r}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(233)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(234)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g_{r}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}}}}\right\|_{{L}^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{(\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(\cdot)}}}\right\|_{\infty}

(235)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|,

(236)

where
\|\cdot\|_{{L}^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
is defined by
\|f\|_{{L}^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|f(x_{i})}}\right|
.
To bound the term
235
, we apply Cauchy-Schwarz to
g_{r}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{n})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})
to obtain

\textstyle\|g_{r}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\|g_{r}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\sqrt{\mathrm{Mass}_{A}(r)}\leq\|g\|_{A,L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\sqrt{\mathrm{Mass}_{A}(r)}.

(237)

Next, we bound the term
236
. For any
x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}
and
x_{i}
satisfying
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{i}-x_{0}^{\prime}}}}\right\|\geq r
and scalars
a,b\in[0,1]
such that
a+b=1
, either
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x^{\star}-x_{i}}}}\right\|\geq ar
or
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x^{\star}-x_{0}^{\prime}}}}\right\|>br
. Hence,

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0})-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x_{0}^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|

(238)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x_{0}\|\geq r}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|

(239)

\textstyle\qquad\leq\underbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x_{i}-x^{\star}}}}\right\|\geq ar}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|}_{\triangleq T_{1}}

(240)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x^{\star}-x_{0}}}}\right\|>br}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|}_{\triangleq T_{2}}.,

(241)

where we define
\delta_{x_{0}}(z)\triangleq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{z=x_{0}}}}\right]
and
\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(z)\triangleq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{z=x_{0}^{\prime}}}}\right]
.

Bounding
240
.

Exchanging the order of the summations and applying Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\textstyle T_{1}

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x^{\star}-x_{i}\|\geq ar}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x^{\star},x_{i})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|

(242)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\star}\|\geq ar}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x^{\star},x_{i})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|

(243)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\star}\|\geq ar}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})^{2}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(244)

\textstyle\qquad\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\star}\|\geq ar}}}\right]\mathbf{k}^{2}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star})}}\right|

(245)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\star}\|\geq ar}}}\right]g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})^{2}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(246)

\textstyle\qquad\cdot\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{i}-x^{\prime}\|\geq ar}}}\right]\mathbf{k}^{2}(x_{i},x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star})}}\right|

(247)

\textstyle=\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\|g\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\tau_{\mathbf{k}}(ar)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star})}}\right|

(248)

\textstyle=2\|g\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\tau_{\mathbf{k}}(ar)

(249)

Bounding
241
.

Rearranging the terms and applying Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\textstyle T_{2}

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x^{\star}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|>br}}}\right]\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})(\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star}))}}\right|

(250)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x^{\star}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|>br}}}\right]\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{r}^{(c)}(x_{i})\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x^{\star})\mathbf{1}_{A}(x_{i})}}\right|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star})}}\right|

(251)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{x^{\star}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x^{\star}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|>br}}}\right]\cdot\|g\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

(252)

\textstyle\qquad\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\delta_{x_{0}}(x^{\star})-\delta_{x_{0}^{\prime}}(x^{\star})}}\right|

(253)

\textstyle=\|g\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x_{0},x_{0}^{\prime}}}\right\}}\|\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\cdot\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|>br}}}\right].

(254)

Appendix C
Proof of
Thm.

2
:
Theorem 2

(KT-KRR for finite-dimensional RKHS)
.

We rely on the localized Gaussian/Rademacher analysis of KRR from prior work
[
29
]
.
Define the
Gaussian critical radius

\varepsilon_{n}>0
to be the smallest positive solution to the inequality

\textstyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}(\varepsilon;\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{H}}(3))\leq\frac{R}{2\sigma}\varepsilon^{2},\quad\text{where}\quad\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}(\varepsilon;\mathcal{F})\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{w}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f}}}\right\|_{n}\leq\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}f(x_{i})}}\right|}}}\right],

(255)

\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{H}}(3)
is the
\|{\cdot}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
-ball of radius 3 and
w_{i}\overset{\textrm{\tiny{i.i.d.} }}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Assumption 4
.

Assume that
\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{k}}(R)
and
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{k}}(c_{\dagger}R)
, for some constant
c_{\dagger}>0
.

Note that for any
g\in\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{k}}(c_{\dagger}R)
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\langle{g},{\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\|{\mathbf{k}(\cdot,x)}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\leq c_{\dagger}R\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\triangleq B,

(256)

where step (i) follows from Cauchy-Schwarz. Thus, the function class
\mathbb{B}_{\mathbf{k}}(c_{\dagger}R)
is
B
-uniformly bounded.
Now define the
Rademacher critical radius

\delta_{n}>0
to be the smallest positive solution to the inequality

\textstyle\mathcal{R}_{n}(\delta;\mathcal{H})\leq\delta^{2},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathcal{R}_{n}(\delta;\mathcal{F})\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{x,\nu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu_{i}f(x_{i})}}\right|}}}\right]

(257)

and
\nu_{i}=\pm 1
each with probability
1/2
.

Finally, we use the following shorthand to control the KT approximation error term,

\textstyle\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}})),\quad\text{where}\quad

(258)

\textstyle\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}

\textstyle\triangleq\max_{x\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x}}}\right\|_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{a}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty,\mathrm{in}}+Y_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}

(259)

and
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}}
is an
inflaction factor

defined in
41
that scales with the covering number
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}
(see
Def.

2
).
With these definitions in place, we are ready to state a detailed version of
Thm.

2
:

Theorem 4

(KT-KRR for finite-dimensional RKHS, detailed)
.

Suppose the kernel operator associated with
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbb{P}
has eigenvalues
\mu_{1}\geq\ldots\geq\mu_{m}>0
(by Mercer’s theorem). Define
C_{m}\triangleq 1/\mu_{m}
.
Let
\varepsilon_{n}
and
\delta_{n}
denote the solutions to
257
and
255
, respectively. Further assume
5
5

5

Note that when
\mathbf{k}
is finite-rank, this condition is automatically satisfied.

\textstyle n\delta_{n}^{2}>\log(4\log(1/\delta_{n})).

(260)

Let
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
denote the
KT-KRR
estimator with regularization parameter

\textstyle\lambda^{\prime}\geq 2\xi_{n}^{2}\quad\text{where}\quad\xi_{n}\triangleq\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n}\vee 4\sqrt{C}_{m}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}.

(261)

Then with probability at least
1-2\delta-2e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c_{1}(b^{2}+\sigma^{2})}}
, we have

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}}}\right\}\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+c\delta_{n}^{2}.

(262)

where recall
\delta
is the success probability of
KT-Compress++

33
.

See
App.

D
for the proof.

We set
\lambda^{\prime}=2\xi_{n}^{2}
, so that
262
becomes

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 3c\xi_{n}^{2}\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+c\delta_{n}^{2}.

(263)

It remains to bound the quantities
\varepsilon_{n}

255
,
\delta_{n}

257
, and
\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

258
. We claim that

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}

\textstyle\leq c_{0}\frac{\sigma}{R}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}

(264)

\textstyle\delta_{n}

\textstyle\leq c_{1}b\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}

(265)

\textstyle\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle\leq c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{m\cdot\log n_{\mathrm{out}}\cdot\log(1/\delta)}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}.

(266)

for some universal positive constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}
.
Now set

\textstyle R=\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\quad\text{}\quad\delta=e^{-1/R^{4}}.

(267)

Thus, we have

\textstyle\xi_{n}\leq c^{\prime}(\frac{\sigma}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}\vee b\vee\frac{4\sqrt{C_{m}}}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}})\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{n}\wedge n_{\mathrm{out}}}.

(268)

for some universal positive constant
c^{\prime}
. Substituting this into
263
leads to the advertised bound
26
.

Proof of claim
264
.

For finite rank kernels,
\hat{\mu}_{j}=0
for
j>m
. Thus, we have
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\varepsilon^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{m\varepsilon^{2}}
. From the critical radius condition
255
, we want
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{m\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{R}{4\sigma}\varepsilon^{2}
, so we may set
\varepsilon_{n}\simeq\frac{\sigma}{R}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}
.

Proof of claim
265
.

By similar logic as above, we have
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\delta^{2},\mu_{j}}}\right\}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{m\delta^{2}}
. From the critical radius condition
257
, we want
\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{m\delta^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{b}\delta^{2}
, so we may set
\delta_{n}\simeq b\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}
.

Proof of claim
266
.

Consider the linear operator
T:\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
that maps a function to the coefficients in the vector space spanned by
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\phi_{i}}}\right\}_{i=1}^{m}
. Note that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{T}}}\right\|=\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{Tf}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}\leq\sqrt{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}

(269)

Since the image of
T
has dimension
m
, we have
\mathrm{rank}({T})\leq m
. Moreover,
\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{1}\cdot\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}^{2}
.
Now we can invoke
[
25
, Eq. 14]
with
\epsilon=\mathfrak{a}/n_{\mathrm{out}}
to obtain

\textstyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{B}_{2}^{d}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}),\mathfrak{a}/n_{\mathrm{out}})\leq\mathcal{N}(T,\mathfrak{a}/n_{\mathrm{out}})\leq(1+\mu_{1}\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}^{2}n_{\mathrm{out}}/\mathfrak{a})^{m}.

(270)

Taking the log on both sides and substituting this bound into
258
, we have

\textstyle\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle=\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}))

(271)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\log\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{B}_{2}^{d}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}),\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}})}}\right]})

(272)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+m\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{T}}}\right\|n_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}}}}\right)}}\right]})

(273)

\textstyle\leq c\frac{\sqrt{m\cdot\log n_{\mathrm{out}}\cdot\log(1/\delta)}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}

(274)

for some positive constant
c
that doesn’t depend on
m,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta
.

Appendix D
Proof of
Thm.

4
:
Theorem 4

(KT-KRR for finite-dimensional RKHS, detailed)
.

We rescale our observation model
2
by
\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
, so that the noise variance is
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sigma/\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}}\right)^{2}
and our new regression function satisfies
\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
. Our final prediction error should then be multiplied by
\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}
to recover a result for the original problem. For simplicity, denote

\textstyle\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma/\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}.

(275)

For notational convenience, define an event

\textstyle\mathcal{E}=\big\{\|\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\big\},

(276)

and our goal is to show that
\mathcal{E}
occurs with high-probability in terms of
{\mathbb{P}}
, the probability regarding all the randomness.
For that end, we introduce several events that are used throughout,

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\triangleq\bigg\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\|{g}\|_{n}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\big|\leq\frac{\delta_{n}}{2}\bigg\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}>\delta_{n}}}\right\},

(277)

where
\delta_{n}
is defined in
257
and
\mathcal{H}
is the RKHS generated by
\mathbf{k}
hence star-shaped. Further, we introduce two technical events
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u),\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}
defined in
334
and
302
respectively, which are proven to occur with small probability, and define a shorthand

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\triangleq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}(\xi_{n})\cap\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}.

(278)

Equipped with these shorthands, observe the following inequality,

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})

\textstyle={\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c})

(279)

\textstyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c})

(280)

where the second inequality is because
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}
due to the assumption
\lambda^{\prime}\geq 2\xi_{n}^{2}\geq 2\delta_{n}^{2}
.

If we are able to show the set inclusion
\{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\}\subseteq\{\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\}
and that
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c})
is small,
we are able refine
280
to the following

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})\geq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c})\geq 1-{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c})-{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}})=1-{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c}),

(281)

where the last quantity
1-{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c})
would be large.

To complete this proof strategy, we claim the set inclusion

\textstyle\{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\}\subseteq\{\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\}

(282)

to hold and prove it in
Sec.

D.1
and further claim

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c})\leq c^{\prime\prime}\big\{\delta+e^{-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/(B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}\big\}

(283)

which verify in
Sec.

D.2
.

Putting the pieces together, claims
282
and
283
collectively implies

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-c^{\prime\prime}\big\{\delta+e^{-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/(B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}\big\}

(284)

as desired.

D.1
Proof of claim
282

There are several intermediary steps we take to show the set inclusion of interest
282
. We introduce the shorthand

\textstyle\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\triangleq\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}.

(285)

By invoking Propositions and basic inequalities to come, we successively show the following chain of set inclusions

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}

\textstyle\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\cap\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}

(286)

\textstyle\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\cap\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}

(287)

\textstyle\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\cap\mathcal{E}

(288)

\textstyle\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}\cap\mathcal{E}.

(289)

Note that step
289
is achieved trivially by dropping
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}
. Further note that
287
is the crucial intermediary step after which we may apply uniform concentration across
n
independent samples. Proof of
287
leverages on the Proposition to come (
proposition

1
) that allows
\|\cdot\|_{n}
and
\|\cdot\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}
to be exchangeable for finite rank kernels.

Recovering step
286

Since
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
and
f^{\star}
are optimal and feasible, respectively for the central optimization problem of interest

\textstyle\min_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})}\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}-f(x_{i}^{\prime})}}\right)^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2},

(290)

we have the basic inequality

\textstyle\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}-\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})}}\right)^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}-f^{\star}(x_{i}^{\prime})}}\right)^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2},

(291)

With some algebra
, may refine
291
to

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}

\textstyle\leq\Big|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\Big|+\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}}\right\}.

(292)

where
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}=\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}
.Suppose that
\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}<\xi_{n}
, then we trivially recover
286
by adding
\lambda^{\prime}>0
. Thus, we assume that
\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\geq\xi_{n}
.

Under the assumption
\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\geq\xi_{n}
, which is without loss of generality, we utilize the basic inequality
292
and control its stochastic component

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|,

(293)

with a careful case work to follow, which is technical by nature.

Case where
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2
: Under such case, we introduce a technical event

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u)

\textstyle\triangleq\bigg\{\exists g\in\mathcal{F}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{2}(\delta_{n})\cap\{\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\geq u\}~\text{such that}~\Big|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\Big|\geq 3\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}u\bigg\},

(294)

for any star-shaped function class
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{H}
. Since
\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
, triangle inequality implies
\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 3
. Moreover, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}
(
\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}
), we have
\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{2}>\delta_{n}
. Thus, we may apply
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}
to the event
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}(\xi_{n})
with
\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{H}}(3)
(i.e., the
\mathcal{H}
-ball of radius 3) to attain

\textstyle\Big|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\Big|\leq c_{0}\xi_{n}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}~\text{on the event}~\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}(\xi_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}.

(295)

Upper bounding the stochastic component of the basic inequality
292
by
295
and dropping the
-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}
term in
292
, we have

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq c_{0}\xi_{n}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+\lambda^{\prime}.

(296)

As a last step under the case
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2
, apply the quadratic formula (specifically, if
a,b\geq 0
and
x^{2}-ax-b\leq 0
, then
x\leq a^{2}+b
) to obtain

\textstyle\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq 4c_{0}^{2}\xi_{n}^{2}+2\lambda^{\prime}.

(297)

Case where
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>2
: Under such case, by assumption we have
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>2>1\geq\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
. Thus, we may derive the following

\textstyle\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}<0\quad\text{and}\quad\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>1,

(298)

which further implies the following inequality

\textstyle\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}=\{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\}\{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\}\leq\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}.

(299)

Further writing
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}=f^{\star}+\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}
and noting that
\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
holds through triangle inequality, we may further refine
299
as

\textstyle\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2-\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}},

(300)

so that the basic inequality in
292
reduces to

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq\Big|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\Big|+\lambda^{\prime}\{2-\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\}.

(301)

We again introduce a technical event that controls the stochastic component of
301
, which is

\textstyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}\triangleq\bigg\{\exists g\in\mathcal{F}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{2}(\delta_{n})\cap\{\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\geq 1\}

\textstyle:

(302)

\textstyle\Big|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\Big|

\textstyle>4\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\frac{1}{4}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\bigg\},

(303)

for a star-shaped function class
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{H}
.

By triangle inequality, we have
\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\geq\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}-\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>1
, and on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}
(
\subset\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}
), we have
\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{2}>\delta_{n}
. Thus, we may apply
g=\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}
to the event
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}
, and the resulting refined basic inequality is

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}

\textstyle\leq 4\xi_{n}\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+(2\xi_{n}^{2}-\lambda^{\prime})\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+2\lambda^{\prime}

(304)

\textstyle\leq 4\xi_{n}\|{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\lambda^{\prime}\quad\text{on the event}~\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}}

(305)

where the second inequality is due to the assumption that
\lambda^{\prime}\geq 2\xi_{n}^{2}
.
We apply the quadratic formula (specifically, if
a,b\geq 0
and
x^{2}-ax-b\leq 0
, then
x\leq a^{2}+b
) to obtain

\textstyle\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq 4c_{0}^{2}\xi_{n}^{2}+2\lambda^{\prime}.

(306)

Putting the pieces together, we have shown

\textstyle\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\quad\text{on the event}~\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}(\xi_{n})\cap\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}},

(307)

which is sufficient to recover
286
.

Recovering step
287

We now upgrade events

\textstyle\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq c^{\prime}(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}

(308)

by exploiting the events
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
(subset of
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}
) that were otherwise not used when recovering
286
. For this end, the following result is a crucial ingredient, which shows that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{n}
are essentially exchangeable with high-probability,

Proposition 1

(Multiplicative guarantee for
KT-Compress++
with
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{RR}}
)
.

Let
C_{m}\triangleq 1/\mu_{m}
and suppose
\delta_{n}
satisfies
260
. Then on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
, where
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
are defined in
33
and
277
respectively, we have

\textstyle(1-4C_{m}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})\|{g}\|_{n}\leq\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\leq(1+4C_{m}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})\|{g}\|_{n}

(309)

uniformly over all
g\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}>\delta_{n}
.

See
Sec.

D.3
for the proof.

An immediate consequence of
proposition

1
is that

\displaystyle\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\leq c(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq c^{\prime}(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}

(310)

on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{lower}},
, which is sufficient to recover
287
.

Recovering step
288

Our last step is to show

\textstyle\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq c^{\prime}(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\{\|\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq c^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime})\}.

(311)

Such result can be immediately shown on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
by observing that
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{KT}}\in\mathcal{H}
, by our assumption that
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}
and by the definition

\textstyle\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{}}{argmin}_{f\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})}L_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(f)+\lambda^{\prime}\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}.

(312)

D.2
Proof of claim
283

It suffices to show the appropriate bounds for the following four probability terms

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\xi_{n})),\quad{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}),\quad{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{c}),\quad{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c}).

(313)

Fix the shorthand

\textstyle B_{\mathcal{H}}\triangleq\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}R^{2}<\infty.

(314)

We know from
[
10
]
that
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c}|\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})\leq\delta
and then we may apply
[
29
, Thm. 14.1]
to obtain a high probability statement,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{c}}}}\right)\leq e^{-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}.

(315)

Now we present two Lemmas that bound the
{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})
probability of events
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\xi_{n})
and
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}
,

Lemma 7

(Controlling bad event when
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2
)
.

Suppose
u\geq\xi_{n}
.
Then for some constant
c>0
,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u)\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\leq\delta+e^{-cn\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}+e^{-cnu^{2}/\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}

(316)

where
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma/\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
.

See
Sec.

D.4
for the proof.

Note that by plugging in
\xi_{n}
into
316
results in a probability that depends on
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
(as
\xi_{n}
depends on
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
). By invoking the definition of
\xi_{n}
, we may further refine the probability bound of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\xi_{n})
by

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\xi_{n})\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\leq\delta+e^{-cn\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}+e^{-cn\delta_{n}^{2}/\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}

(317)

Lemma 8

(Controlling bad event when
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>2
)
.

For some constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\leq\delta+e^{-cn\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}+ce^{-n\xi_{n}^{2}/(c^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}

(318)

where
\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma/\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
.

See
Sec.

D.5
for the proof.

It is notable that
\xi_{n}
in the probability bound of
318
contains a term
\varepsilon_{n}
defined in
255
that is a function of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
. Invoking the definition of
\xi_{n}
, we observe the probability upper bound
318
can be refined to

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\leq\delta+e^{-cn\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}+ce^{-n\delta_{n}^{2}/(c^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})},

(319)

which does not depend on
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
.

Putting the pieces together, we have the following probability bound for some constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
,

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})

\textstyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(\xi_{n})\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}})

(320)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unifconc-prob-bound}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:case-I-bound-refined}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:case-II-bound-refined}}}{{\leq}}c\big\{\delta+e^{-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/(B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}\big\}

(321)

thereby implying
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{good}}^{c})\leq c^{\prime\prime}\big\{\delta+e^{-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/(B_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\wedge\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}\big\}
for some constant
c^{\prime\prime}
.

D.3
Proof of
proposition

1
:
Proposition 1

(Multiplicative guarantee for
KT-Compress++
with
\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{RR}}
)
.

Fix
g\in\mathcal{H}
. Denote
\langle{g},{h}\rangle=\int g(x)h(x)dx
as the inner product in the
L^{2}
sense. By Mercer’s theorem
[
29
, Cor. 12.26]
, the
\mathbf{k}
-norm of
g
has a basis expansion
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle{g},{\phi_{i}}\rangle^{2}/\lambda_{i}
so that

\textstyle\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle{g},{\phi_{i}}\rangle^{2}/\lambda_{m}=C_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\quad\text{since}\quad C_{m}=1/\lambda_{m}.

(322)

The assumption
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}
implies that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}

277
, we have

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n}\leq\|{g}\|_{n}

(323)

Moreover,
g
must be a non-zero function.
Note that
g^{2}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathrm{RR}})
(see
Sec.

F.3
).
Thus, we may apply
Lem.

14
to
f_{1}=f_{2}=g
and
a=1,b=0
to obtain

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\|{g}\|_{n}^{2}-\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}}}\right|=\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g^{2}(x_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}g^{2}(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\leq\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}.

(324)

The LHS can be expanded as

\textstyle\big|\|{g}\|_{n}^{2}-\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\big|=\big|\|{g}\|_{n}-\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\big|\cdot\underbrace{\big|\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\big|}_{>0\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unif_conc_result}}}.

(325)

Thus, we may rearrange
324
and combine with
322
to obtain

\textstyle\big|\|{g}\|_{n}-\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\big|\leq\frac{C_{m}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}.

(326)

On event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unif_conc_event}}}{{\leq}}(\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n})^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{4}+\delta_{n}\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n}^{2}.

(327)

Thus, we have

\textstyle\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:twonorm-g-upper-bound}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}}\right|}+\frac{\delta_{n}\|{g}\|_{n}}{\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}+\frac{\|{g}\|_{n}^{2}}{\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}

(328)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unif_conc_result}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{2\delta_{n}}+\delta_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n}\cdot\frac{\|{g}\|_{n}}{\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}

(329)

\textstyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n}

(330)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unif_conc_result}}}{{\leq}}3\|{g}\|_{n}+\|{g}\|_{n}=4\|{g}\|_{n}.

(331)

Using
331
to refine
326
, we have on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
:

\textstyle\big|\|{g}\|_{n}-\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\big|\leq 4C_{m}\|{g}\|_{n}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}.

(332)

With some algebra, this implies with probability at least
1-\delta-\exp(-c^{\prime}n\delta_{n}^{2}/B_{\mathcal{F}}^{2})
:

\textstyle(1-4C_{m}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})\|{g}\|_{n}\leq\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\leq(1+4C_{m}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})\|{g}\|_{n}

(333)

uniformly over all non-zero
g\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}>\delta_{n}
.

D.4
Proof of
Lem.

7
:
Lemma 7

(Controlling bad event when
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq 2
)
.

Recall
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
defined by
33
and
277
. Also recall that
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
combined with the assumption
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}
invokes the event
309
.
Our aim is to show

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\subseteq\{Z_{n}(2u)\geq 2u^{2}\},\quad\text{where}\quad Z_{n}(t)\triangleq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}:\\
\|g\|_{n}\leq t\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}g(x_{i})}}\right|

(334)

so that we have a probability bound

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u))\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(Z_{n}(2u)\geq 2u^{2}).

(335)

The first RHS term can be bounded by
\delta
(see
33
). The second RHS term can bounded by
315
. The third term can be bounded by

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(Z_{n}(2u)\geq 2u^{2})=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({Z_{n}(u)\geq u^{2}/2+u^{2}/2}}}\right)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({Z_{n}(u)\geq u\varepsilon_{n}/2+u^{2}/2}}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}e^{-\frac{nu^{2}}{8\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}},

(336)

where (i) follows from our assumption that
u\geq\varepsilon_{n}
and (ii) follows from applying generic concentration bounds on
Z_{n}(u)
(see
[
29
, Thm. 2.26, Eq. 13.66]
).
Putting together the pieces yields our desired probability bound
316
.

Proof of claim
334
.

Consider the event
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}(u)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
. The norm equivalence established on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
in
proposition

1
is an important ingredient throughout.

Let
g\in\mathcal{H}
be the function that satisfies three conditions:
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}

,
\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\geq u
, and

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\geq 3\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}u.

(337)

Define the normalized function

\textstyle\widetilde{g}=u\cdot g/\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}

(338)

so that it satisfies
\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}=u
and also

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widetilde{g}(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\geq 3u^{2}.

(339)

By triangle inequality, the LHS of
339
can be further upper bounded by

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}\widetilde{g}(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\widetilde{g}(x_{i})\bigg|+\frac{u}{\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})}}\right|.

(340)

Recall the chosen
g
satisies
\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\geq u
. Observe that

\textstyle v_{i}g(x_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:process}}}{{=}}(y_{i}-f^{\star}(x_{i}))g(x_{i})=-f^{\star}(x_{i})g(x_{i})+y_{i}g(x_{i}),

(341)

so we may apply
Lem.

14
with
f_{1}=f^{\star},f_{2}=g
and
a=-1,b=1
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{conc}}
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})}}\right|\leq\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}.

(342)

Thus, we may rearrange
340
and combine with
339
and
342
to obtain

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\widetilde{g}(x_{i})}}\right|\geq 3u^{2}-\frac{u}{\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

(343)

Note that

\textstyle\frac{\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}=\frac{\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|g\|_{2}}\cdot\frac{\|g\|_{2}}{\|g\|_{n}}\cdot\frac{\|g\|_{n}}{\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}.

(344)

We tackle each term in turn. First,
\frac{\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|g\|_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:knorm-twonorm}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{C}_{m}
. Since we assume
\|g\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}
, we have
\frac{\|g\|_{2}}{\|g\|_{n}}\leq\frac{\delta_{n}/2+\|g\|_{n}}{\|g\|_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:unif_conc_result}}}{{\leq}}2
on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
; and
\frac{\|g\|_{n}}{\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:nnorm-noutnorm}}}{{\leq}}2
on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
. Taken together,

\textstyle\frac{\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\leq 4\sqrt{C}_{m}\quad\text{on event}\quad\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}.

(345)

As
u\geq\xi_{n}\geq 4\sqrt{C}_{m}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}
by assumption,
we have therefore found
\widetilde{g}
with norm
\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}=u
satisfying

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\widetilde{g}(x_{i})\bigg|\geq 3u^{2}-u^{2}=2u^{2}.

(346)

We may further show that

\textstyle\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{n}=\frac{u}{\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\|{g}\|_{n}\leq u\frac{\|{g}\|_{n}}{\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\leq u\cdot 2\quad\text{on event}\quad\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta},

(347)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}
and by applying
355
.
So we observe

\textstyle 2u^{2}\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\widetilde{g}(x_{i})\bigg|\leq\sup_{\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{n}\leq 2u}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\widetilde{g}(x_{i})\bigg|=Z_{n}(2u)

(348)

D.5
Proof of
Lem.

8
:
Lemma 8

(Controlling bad event when
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}>2
)
.

Our aim is to show for any
g\in\partial\mathcal{H}

with
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\geq 1
,

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\leq 2\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\frac{1}{16}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\quad\text{with high probability.}

(349)

Note that it is sufficient to prove our aim for
g\in\partial\mathcal{H}
with
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
—by proving only for
g
with
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
, then for any
h\in\partial\mathcal{H}
with
\|{h}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\geq 1
, we may plug
g=h/\|{h}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
into

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|\leq 2\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}\quad

(350)

to recover the aim of interest. So without loss of generality, we show
350
for all
g
such that
g\in\partial\mathcal{H}
and
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}
denote the event where
350
is violated, i.e. there exists
g\in\partial\mathcal{H}
with
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
so that

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|>3\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}.

(351)

We prove the following set inclusion,

\textstyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{conc}}

(352)

\textstyle\subseteq

\textstyle\bigg\{\exists\ g\in\partial\mathcal{H}~\text{s.t.}~\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1~\text{and}~\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})\bigg|>2\varepsilon_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}+2\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}^{2}\bigg\},

(353)

where we know the RHS event of
353
has probability bounded by
ce^{-n\xi_{n}^{2}/(c^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})}
which is proven in
[
29
, Lem. 13.23]
.
So the set inclusion
353
implies a bound over the event
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}},

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}})\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{conc}}^{c})+ce^{-n\xi_{n}^{2}/(c^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}^{2})},

(354)

where
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}^{c})\leq\delta
by
33
and
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{c})
by
315
.

Choose
g
so that
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
and
351
holds. Condition
\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}=1
as well as the condition
322
resulting from a finite rank kernel
\mathbf{k}
implies
\delta_{n}\leq 1\leq\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq\sqrt{C_{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}
.
Invoke
proposition

1
for the choice of
g
that satisfies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{2}\geq\delta_{n}/\sqrt{C_{m}}\geq\delta_{n}
, so that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
, we have the following norm equivalence,

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}\leq\|{g}\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}\quad\text{for any $n$ such that $C_{m}\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}\leq 1/18$.}

(355)

Then we have the following chain of inequalities, which holds on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})\bigg|

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|-\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}v_{i}^{\prime}g(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigg|

(356)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\geq}}3\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}-\|{g}\|_{\mathbf{k}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

(357)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:knorm-nnoutnorm}}}{{\geq}}3\xi_{n}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{2}-4\sqrt{C_{m}}\|g\|_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

(358)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:nnorm-noutnorm}}}{{\geq}}(\frac{3}{2}\xi_{n}-2\sqrt{C_{m}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}^{2},

(359)

where step (i) follows from triangle inequality and step (ii) follows from our assumption
351
to bound the first term and our approximation guarantee
342
to bound the second term.
By definition of
\xi_{n}
, we have

\textstyle\frac{3}{2}\xi_{n}-2\sqrt{C_{m}}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}\geq 2\xi_{n}.

(360)

Using this to refine
359
, we have

\textstyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}g(x_{i})\bigg|

\textstyle\geq 2\xi_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}+2\xi_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{n}^{2},

(361)

which directly implies the inclusion
353
as desired.

Appendix E
Proof of
Thm.

3
:
Theorem 3

(
KT-KRR
guarantee for infinite-dimensional RKHS)
.

We state a more detailed version of the theorem:

Theorem 5

(
KT-KRR
guarantee for infinite-dimensional RKHS, detailed)
.

Assume
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})
and
Assumption

1
is satisfied. If
\mathbf{k}
is
\textsc{LogGrowth}(\alpha,\beta)
,
then for some constant
c
(depending on
d,\alpha,\beta
),
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
with
\lambda^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}(1/n_{\mathrm{out}})
satisfies

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{\log^{\alpha}n_{\mathrm{out}}}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}

(362)

with probability at least
1-2\delta-2e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c_{1}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}}
.

If
\mathbf{k}
is
\textsc{PolyGrowth}(\alpha,\beta)
with
\alpha\in(0,2)
, then for some constant
c
(depending on
d,\alpha,\beta
),
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
with
\lambda=\mathcal{O}(n_{\mathrm{out}}^{-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}})
satisfies

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}

\textstyle\leq c\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}n_{\mathrm{out}}^{-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}\log n_{\mathrm{out}}+c^{\prime}b^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}

(363)

with probability at least
1-2\delta-2e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c_{1}(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}}
.

E.1
Generic KT-KRR guarantee

We state a generic result for infinite-dimensional RKHS that only depends on the Rademacher and Gaussian critical radii as well as the KT approximation term, all introduced in
App.

C
.

Theorem 6

(
KT-KRR
)
.

Let
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{k})

and
Assumption

1
is satisfied. Let
\delta_{n}
,
\varepsilon_{n}
denote the solutions to
255
,
257
, respectively. Denote
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
with regularization parameter
\lambda^{\prime}\geq 2\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}
, where
\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}
is defined by
258
.
Then with probability at least
1-2\delta-2e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}}-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{n\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}
, we have

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{full}}+\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{KT}},\quad\text{where}\quad

(364)

\textstyle\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{full}}

\textstyle\triangleq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}+c\delta_{n}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad

(365)

\textstyle\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{KT}}

\textstyle\triangleq c\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}~\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}.

(366)

See
App.

F
for the proof.

The term
\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{full}}
follows from the excess risk bound of
Full-KRR

\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}
. The term
\mathbb{U}^{\mathrm{KT}}
follows from our KT approximation.
Clearly, the best rates are achieved when we choose
\lambda=2\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}
.

E.2
Proof of explicit rates

The strategy for each setting is as follows:

1.

Bound the Gaussian critical radius
257
using
[
29
, Cor. 13.18]
, which reduces to finding
\varepsilon>0
satisfying the inequality

\textstyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\varepsilon^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}\leq\beta\varepsilon^{2},\quad\text{where}\quad\beta\triangleq\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{4\sigma}

(367)

and
\hat{\mu}_{1}\geq\hat{\mu}_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\hat{\mu}_{n}\geq 0
are the eigenvalues of the normalized kernel matrix
\mathbf{K}/n
, where
\mathbf{K}
is defined by
8
.

2.

Bound the Rademacher critical radius
255
using
[
29
, Cor. 14.5]
, which reduces to solving the inequality

\textstyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\delta^{2},\mu_{j}}}\right\}}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{b},

(368)

where
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mu_{j}}}\right)_{j=1}^{\infty}
are the eigenvalues of the
\mathbf{k}
according to Mercer’s theorem
[
29
, Thm. 12.20]
and
b
is the uniform bound on the function class.

3.

Bound
\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

258
using the covering number bound
\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{B}_{2}^{d}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}),\epsilon)
from
Assumption

3
.

In the sequel, we make use of the following notation. Let

\textstyle R_{n}\triangleq 1+\sup_{x\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}\|{x_{i}}\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:radius}}}{{=}}1+\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}\quad\text{and}\quad L_{\mathbf{k}}(r)\triangleq\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{d}}{\log 2}r^{\beta}

(369)

according to
[
16
, Eq. 6]
, where
\mathfrak{C}_{d}
is the constant that appears in
Assumption

3
.

E.2.1
Proof of
363

We begin by solving
367
.

Lemma 9

(Critical Gaussian radius for
PolyGrowth
kernels)
.

Suppose
Assumption

1
is satisfied and
\mathbf{k}
is
PolyGrowth
with
\alpha<2
as defined by
Assumption

3
. Then the Gaussian critical radius satisfies

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\simeq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2c}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}/4\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})(1+\frac{32\alpha}{2-\alpha})}}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}.

(370)

Proof.

[
16
, Cor. B.1]
implies that

\textstyle\hat{\mu}_{j}\leq 4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}{j-1}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\quad\text{for all}\quad j>L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})+1

(371)

Let
k
be the smallest integer such that

\textstyle k>L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})+1\quad\text{and}\quad 4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}{k-1}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}.

(372)

By
Assumption

1
,
R_{n}
is a constant, so the first inequality is easily satisfied for large enough
n

\textstyle k\geq 2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\varepsilon^{-\alpha}+1.

(373)

Then

\textstyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\varepsilon^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{k\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}{j-1}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}

(374)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{k\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{4L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{2/\alpha}}{2/\alpha-1}k^{1-2/\alpha}}

(375)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:k-cond}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\varepsilon^{2-\alpha}+\frac{4\cdot 2^{2-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}{2/\alpha-1}\varepsilon^{2-\alpha}},

(376)

where step (i) follows from the approximation

\textstyle\sum_{j=k}^{n-1}4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}{j}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\leq 4L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{2/\alpha}\int_{k}^{\infty}t^{-2/\alpha}dt=4L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{2/\alpha}\frac{1}{2/\alpha-1}k^{1-\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

(377)

To solve
367
, it suffices to solve

\textstyle\frac{2c}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})(1+\frac{16}{2/\alpha-1})\varepsilon^{2-\alpha}}\leq\beta\varepsilon^{2}

(378)

\textstyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad

\textstyle\varepsilon\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2c}{\beta}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})(1+\frac{32\alpha}{2-\alpha})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\cdot n^{-\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}.

(379)

Since
\varepsilon_{n}
is the smallest such solution to
367
by definition, we have
370
as desired.
∎

We proceed to solve
368
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose
Assumption

1
is satisfied and
\mathbf{k}
is
PolyGrowth
with
\alpha<2
as defined by
Assumption

3
. Then the Rademacher critical radius satisfies

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\simeq b^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2^{-\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})(1+\frac{32\alpha}{2-\alpha})}}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}.

(380)

Proof.

Thus, we can solve the following inequality

\textstyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\delta^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}\leq\frac{1}{b}\delta^{2},

(381)

Following the same logic as in the proof of
Lem.

9
but with
\beta=1/b
yields the desired bound.
∎

Finally, it remains to bound
258
. We have

\textstyle\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle=\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}))

(382)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\log\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{B}_{2}^{d}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}),\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}})}}\right]})

(383)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\mathfrak{C}_{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}}}}\right)^{\alpha}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}+1)^{\beta})}}\right]}

(384)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)}+\sqrt{\mathfrak{C}_{d}\frac{(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}+1)^{\beta}}{\mathfrak{a}^{\alpha}}}n_{\mathrm{out}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}}\right]}}\right)

(385)

\textstyle\leq n_{\mathrm{out}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)}\cdot\sqrt{\mathfrak{C}_{d}\frac{(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}+1)^{\beta}}{\mathfrak{a}^{\alpha}}}}}\right)

(386)

for some universal positive constant
c
.

In summary, there exists positive constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}
such that

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\leq c_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}\quad\delta_{n}^{2}\leq c_{1}b^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}\quad\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}\leq c_{2}\mathfrak{a}^{2}n_{\mathrm{out}}^{-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}\log n_{\mathrm{out}}

(387)

Setting
\lambda^{\prime}=c_{2}\mathfrak{a}^{2}n_{\mathrm{out}}^{-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}\log n_{\mathrm{out}}
, we have

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}

\textstyle\leq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}+c^{\prime}\delta_{n}^{2}

(388)

\textstyle\leq c\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}n_{\mathrm{out}}^{-\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}\log n_{\mathrm{out}}+c^{\prime}b^{\frac{4}{2+\alpha}}n^{-\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}.

(389)

E.2.2
Proof of
362

We begin by solving
367
.

Lemma 11

(Critical Gaussian radius for
LogGrowth
kernels)
.

Under
Assumption

1
and
LogGrowth
version of
Assumption

3
, Gaussian critical radius satisfies

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\simeq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\frac{\log(2e\cdot\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{4\sigma}\sqrt{n})^{\alpha}}{n}\cdot L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}

(390)

for some constant
C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}
that only depends on
\alpha
.
where we ignore log-log factors.

Proof.

[
16
, Cor. B.1]
implies that

\textstyle\hat{\mu}_{j}\leq 4\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{j-1}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}}\right)\quad\text{for all}\quad j>L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})+1

(391)

Let
k
be the smallest integer such that

\textstyle k>L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})+1\quad\text{and}\quad 4\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{j-1}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}}\right)\leq\varepsilon^{2}.

(392)

By
Assumption

1
,
R_{n}
is a constant, so the first inequality is easily satisfied for large enough
n

\textstyle k\geq L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}+1.

(393)

Thus,
k=\lceil{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}+1}\rceil
. Then

\textstyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\varepsilon^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{k\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}4\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{j-1}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}}\right)}

(394)

Consider the following approximation:

\textstyle\sum_{\ell=k}^{n-1}4\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\ell}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}}\right)\leq 4e^{2}\int_{k}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{2t}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}^{1/\alpha}}dt=\int_{k-1}^{\infty}c^{t^{1/\alpha}}dt,

(395)

where
c\triangleq\exp(-(L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})/2)^{-1/\alpha})\in(0,1)
. Defining
m\triangleq-\log c>0
and
k^{\prime}\triangleq k-1
, we have

\textstyle\int_{k^{\prime}}^{\infty}c^{t^{1/\alpha}}dt\leq C_{\alpha}(k^{\prime}b^{-1}+b^{\alpha-1}m^{-\alpha})e^{-mk^{\prime 1/\alpha}},

(396)

by
Li et al. [16, Eq. 50]
, where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant satisfying
(x+y)^{\alpha}\leq C_{\alpha}(x^{\alpha}+y^{\alpha})
for any
x,y>0
and
b
is a known constant depending only on
\alpha
. Plugging in
k^{\prime}=\lceil{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}}\rceil
, we can bound the exponential by

\textstyle e^{-mk^{\prime 1/\alpha}}\leq e^{-mL_{k}(R_{n})^{1/\alpha}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{-mL_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{1/\alpha}}.

(397)

Note that we can simplify the exponent by
-mL_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{1/\alpha}=-(L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})/2)^{-1/\alpha}L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})^{1/\alpha}=-2^{1/\alpha}
.
Note that
k^{\prime}=k-1\geq L(R_{n})=2m^{-\alpha}
. Thus, we can absorb the
b^{\alpha-1}m^{-\alpha}
term in
396
into
k
and obtain the following bound

\textstyle\sum_{\ell=k}^{n-1}4\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\ell}{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}}}\right)\leq C_{\alpha}^{\prime}k^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{-2^{1/\alpha}},

(398)

where
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}
depends only on
\alpha
.
Plugging this bound into
394
, we have

\textstyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\varepsilon^{2},\hat{\mu}_{j}}}\right\}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{k\varepsilon^{2}+C_{\alpha}^{\prime}k\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2e}}}\right)^{2^{1/\alpha}}}

(399)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:k-cond-log}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2c}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}(\varepsilon^{2}+C_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2e}}}\right)^{2^{1/\alpha}})}

(400)

\textstyle\leq\frac{2c}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\varepsilon^{2^{1/(1\vee\alpha)}}}

(401)

for some constant
C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}
that only depends on
\alpha
and universal positive constant
c
.
To solve
367
, it suffices to solve

\textstyle\frac{2c}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\varepsilon^{2^{1/(1\vee\alpha)}}}\leq\beta\varepsilon^{2},

(402)

which is implied by the looser bound

\textstyle\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\cdot\frac{4c^{2}}{n}\cdot L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}}}\right)^{-\alpha}.

(403)

The solution to
367
(up to log-log factors) is

\textstyle\varepsilon\simeq\frac{\log(2e\cdot\beta\sqrt{n})^{\alpha/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{4c^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\cdot L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}}.

(404)

∎

We proceed to solve
368
.

Lemma 12

(Critical Gaussian radius for
LogGrowth
kernels)
.

Under
Assumption

1
and
LogGrowth
version of
Assumption

3
, the Rademacher critical radius satisfies

\textstyle\delta_{n}^{2}\simeq b^{2}\frac{\log(\frac{2e}{b}\cdot\sqrt{n})^{\alpha}}{n}\cdot L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{n})C_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}.

(405)

Proof.

Following the same logic as in the proof of
Lem.

11
but with
\beta=1/b
yields the desired bound.
∎

Finally, it remains to bound
258
. We have

\textstyle\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle=\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\mathfrak{W}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,n_{\mathrm{out}},\delta,\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}},\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}))

(406)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\log\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{B}_{2}^{d}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}),\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}})}}\right]})

(407)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(2+\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n_{\mathrm{out}}\log(n/n_{\mathrm{out}})}{\delta}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}}}\right)+\mathfrak{C}_{d}\log(\frac{en_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}})^{\alpha}(\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}+1)^{\beta}}}\right]})

(408)

for some universal positive constant
c
.

In summary, there exists universal positive constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}
such that

\textstyle\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\leq c_{0}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\frac{\log(2e\cdot\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{4\sigma}\sqrt{n})^{\alpha}}{n}\quad\delta_{n}^{2}\leq c_{1}b^{2}\frac{\log(\frac{2e}{b}\cdot\sqrt{n})^{\alpha/2}}{n}\quad\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}\leq c_{2}\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\log(\frac{en_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}})^{\alpha/2}\mathfrak{R}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\beta/2}.

(409)

Setting
\lambda^{\prime}=2c_{2}\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\log(\frac{en_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}})^{\alpha/2}
, we have

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}

\textstyle\leq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}+c^{\prime}\delta_{n}^{2}

(410)

\textstyle\leq c\frac{\log(2e\cdot\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}}{4\sigma}\sqrt{n})^{\alpha}}{n}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1}}\right]^{2}\frac{c}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\log(\frac{en_{\mathrm{out}}}{\mathfrak{a}})^{\alpha/2}+c^{\prime}b^{2}\frac{\log(\frac{2e}{b}\cdot\sqrt{n})^{\alpha/2}}{n}.

(411)

Appendix F
Proof of
Thm.

6
:
Theorem 6

(
KT-KRR
)
.

Our first goal is to bound the in-sample prediction error.
We relate
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{n}^{2}
to
\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}-f^{\star}}\|_{n}^{2}
, where the latter quantity has well known properties from standard analyses of the KRR estimator (refer to
[
29
]
).
Note that regularization parameter
\lambda^{\prime}
of KT based estimator
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
is independently chosen from the regularization parameter
\lambda
of the estimator based on original samples
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}
. For
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}
, we choose the regularization parameter

\textstyle\lambda=2\varepsilon_{n}^{2},

(412)

which is known to yield optimal
L^{2}
error rates.

Define the main event of interest,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\triangleq\{\|\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\leq c(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1]^{2}\}.

(413)

Our goal is to show
\mathcal{E}
occurs with high probability. For that end, we introduce several additional events that are used throughout this proof.

For some constant
c>
, define the event of an appealing in-sample prediction error of
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
,

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(t)\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[t^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}}}\right]\cdot(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)^{2}}}\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad t\geq\varepsilon_{n}.

(414)

where
\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}
is defined in
258
. Recall
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
is the event where
KT-Compress++
succeeds as defined by
33
.

Further as
f^{\star}
and
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}
are both in
\{f\in\mathcal{H}:\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq R\}
, we may deduce that all the functions under consideration satisfies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\leq\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}R
where
\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}<\infty
. Accordingly, we define a uniform concentration event,

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{\prime}\triangleq\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\|{f}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{f}\|_{n}^{2}\big|\leq\|{f}\|_{2}^{2}/2+\delta_{n}^{2}/2\}\quad\text{where}\quad\mathcal{F}=\{f\in\mathcal{H}:\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}R\}.

(415)

Event
415
is analogous to the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}
previously defined in
277
when dealing with finite rank kernels.

We first show that

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}.

(416)

Notice that almost surely we have

\textstyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}R,

(417)

thereby implying

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2~\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\quad\text{on the event $\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{\prime}$.}\quad

(418)

Next invoking the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n})
along with
418
, we have

\textstyle\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{KT},\lambda^{\prime}}-f^{\star}}\|_{2}^{2}

\textstyle\leq 2c[(\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n})^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}]\cdot(\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1)^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}

(419)

\textstyle\leq c(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{\prime}+\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}})[\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+1]^{2}.

(420)

which recovers the event of
\mathcal{E}
.

The remaining task is to show
\mathcal{E}
is of high-probability, which amounts to showing events
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(t)
and
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{\prime}
are of high-probability by reflecting on
416
. From
[
29
, Thm. 14.1]
, we may immediately derive

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{conc}}^{\prime})\geq 1-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}R^{2}}}

(421)

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

We further claim that

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(t)\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{c_{0}\sigma^{2}}}-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{n\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}

(422)

for some constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Proof of claim
422
is deferred to
Sec.

F.1
. Plugging in
t=\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n}
into
422
, and invoking inequality
\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n}\geq\delta_{n}
so as to decouple the dependence on
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n})\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c_{0}\sigma^{2}}}-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}

(423)

which further implies

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(\varepsilon_{n}\vee\delta_{n})}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-e^{-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{c_{0}\sigma^{2}}}-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}.

(424)

Putting the pieces together, for some constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-c_{0}e^{-c_{1}\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\wedge(\sigma^{2}/\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2})\wedge(\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}R^{2})}}.

(425)

Overall,
416
and
425
collectively yields the desired result.

F.1
Proof of claim
422

To prove claim
422
, we introduce two new intermediary and technical events. For some positive constant
c_{0}
, define the event
6
6

6

Since the input points in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}
are fixed, the randomness in
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}
originates entirely from the randomness of the noise variables
\bm{\xi}
.
when in-sample prediction error of
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}
is appealing

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{full},n}(t)\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|{\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{full},\lambda}-f^{\star}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq 3c_{0}\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}t^{2}}}\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad t\geq\varepsilon_{n}.

(426)

The second intermediary event, denoted as
\mathcal{E}_{\widehat{\Delta}_{\textrm{KT}}}(t)
, is the intersection of
459
and
466
, which we do not elaborate here due to its technical nature—event
\mathcal{E}_{\widehat{\Delta}_{\textrm{KT}}}(t)
plays an analogous role to
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{c}
defined in
334
and
302
respectively.

Our goal here is two-folds: first is to show

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{full},n}(t)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\widehat{\Delta}_{\textrm{KT}}}(t)\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},n}(t)

(427)

and second is to prove the following bound

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{full},n}(t)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\widehat{\Delta}_{\textrm{KT}}}(t)\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta-e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{c_{0}\sigma^{2}}}-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{n\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}},

(428)

from which
422
follows. Note that
Wainwright [29, Thm. 13.17]
show

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{full},n}(t)}}}\right)\geq 1-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{n\|{f^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}

(429)

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
and that
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}\mid\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{in}}}}}\right)\geq 1-\delta
. So it remains to bound the probability of event
\mathcal{E}_{\widehat{\Delta}_{\textrm{KT}}}(t)
, which we show below.

Given
f
, define the following quantities

\textstyle L_{n}(f)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f^{2}(x_{i})-2f(x_{i})y_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad

(430)

\textstyle L_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(f)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{1}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(f^{2}(x_{i}^{\prime})-2f(x_{i}^{\prime})y_{i}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}.

(431)

In the sequel, we repeatedly make use of the following fact: on event
\mathcal{E}_{\textrm{KT},\delta}
defined in
33
, we have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|L_{n}(f)-L_{n_{\mathrm{out}}}(f)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|{f}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+2}}\right)\cdot\eta_{n,\mathbf{k}}\quad\text{for all non-zero}\quad f\in\mathcal{H}.

(432)

The claim of
432
is deferred to the end of this section. Given
f
